## Architecture

Joshua Tree is a whole computer written from nothing.

You turn it on and it draws a desktop. You use its apps: notes, mail, a calendar, weather, a chat that can set reminders for you.

Everything it does, it does itself. There is no Linux underneath and no borrowed library.

It runs in three places:
- in your browser, on the landing page
- in QEMU on a Mac
- from a USB stick on a real PC

It is all one file, `kernel.elf`, built from the `boot/`, `kernel/`, `drivers/` and `lib/` folders.

`docs/ROADMAP.md` (Roadmap) is the plan. This page is the map of what exists today.

### How it starts

1. **Something loads it.** QEMU, the browser demo or a real bootloader drops the kernel into memory and jumps to `boot/boot.S`.
2. **It sets up its own memory.** A few lines of assembly move the kernel to where it expects to live, then hand off to C.
3. **It wakes the hardware, one piece at a time.** `kmain` starts the processor tables, the clock, memory, the scheduler and the disk. Each one needs the last.
4. **You land in a shell.** Type `gui` and the desktop starts. The shell and the desktop are the same program. The Terminal is the shell in a window.

### Boot modes

Pass keywords on the multiboot command line to change how the kernel starts. Use QEMU's `-append` flag or the browser emulator's cmdline config.

| Flag | What it does |
|---|---|
| `phone` | Boots into portrait phone mode (430x932) instead of the desktop (960x540 retina). Handled in `kernel/kernel.c`'s `kmain` as `boot_to_phone`, used by `gui_run`. |
| `samantha` | Skips the desktop and opens the ring-3 Samantha program (`user/samantha.c`) first. Handled in `kernel/kernel.c`'s `kmain` as `boot_to_samantha`, which launches dock slot 6. |

### The layers

Each layer only leans on the ones above it on this page, so it reads top to bottom.

#### CPU setup and interrupts

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `boot/boot.S` | The first code that runs. Sets up just enough memory to reach C. |
| `kernel/gdt.c` | Tells the processor what is kernel and what is a program, so programs can safely call into the kernel. |
| `kernel/idt.c` + `kernel/isr.S` | The interrupt table and the 32 CPU exception handlers. A kernel-mode fault paints a panic screen and halts. A user-mode fault kills that one program and the kernel keeps going. Its crash report now names the function that faulted, via `kernel/symtab.h`. |
| `kernel/symtab.h` + `kernel/backtrace.c` | Crash reports with real function names. `kernel/symtab.c` (generated, never committed) is a sorted address-to-name table read from `nm -n` on a first-pass link of the kernel, done twice by the Makefile so the table can live inside the very kernel it describes. `backtrace.c` binary-searches that table for a fault's EIP and walks a few EBP stack frames above it, printing `at <func>+0x<off>` lines over serial and on the panic screen. `kernel/symtab_stub.c` is the empty placeholder table pass one links against, since the real table doesn't exist yet at that point. |
| `kernel/pic.c` | Routes hardware signals, like a key press, so they never get confused with processor errors. |
| `kernel/irq.c` + `kernel/irq_stubs.S` | The hardware interrupt handlers. The timer tick drives the scheduler, the keyboard fills a ring buffer. |
| `kernel/entropy.c` + `kernel/entropy.h` | The kernel's only source of unpredictable bytes: an HMAC_DRBG (BearSSL SHA-256) seeded from RDRAND when the CPU has it, RDTSC jitter and interrupt timing. Password salts draw from it. Boot logs `entropy: sources=` over serial. |
| `kernel/picker.c` + `kernel/picker.h`, `kernel/picker_weights.h`, `kernel/picker_golden.h`, `tools/checks/picker-check.sh` | 3.8.5: a small tool picker in freestanding C, Samantha's hands distilled (turing 10.0 slice). `picker_pick(text)` lowercases, hashes words and letter triples (FNV-1a, 2048 buckets, each feature once) and sums int8 weights per tool, 119 classes with "null" for not-a-command; no floats, no libc, no network. It abstains: the null score carries a lean, and a tool whose lead over the runner-up is under `PICK_MARGIN` becomes null. Weights are trained on Turing's training rows plus about 2,000 chatty not-a-command rows (open questions, and questions that merely mention a tool's topic) and pruned to 17,947 (about 39KB of the kernel's room; `bss-margin-check.py` still passes). The two headers are generated by Turing's training/smallpicker/export_c.py; the golden header holds 297 sentences with the Python reference's picks. Shell: `pick <sentence>` names the tool, `pickcheck` runs the golden table. Check: `picker-check.sh` boots with `-nic none`, needs `pickcheck: 297/297 ok` and four typed sentences picked right, and fails if the C is changed. |
| `kernel/brk.c` | `SYS_BRK` (1.9.27): a per-task heap at `JT_BRK_BASE` grown a frame at a time from the PMM, zeroed, mapped into that task's directory only, capped at 8 MB per task and a 4 MB free-frame floor, released from `syscall_release_task` on exit and crash with a `brk: released` serial line. |
| `kernel/syscall.c` | The `int 0x80` dispatch table. Numbers and calling convention are Linux's, so the ABI needs no translation. The contract is written down in the System call ABI section. |

#### Memory

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `kernel/pmm.c` | Physical memory as a bitmap of 4KB frames, sized from what the bootloader reports. |
| `kernel/paging.c` | Decides which memory each program can see, and keeps programs out of the kernel's. |
| `kernel/kheap.c` | `kmalloc` and `kfree`. A first-fit free list that grows one frame at a time. |
| `lib/text_ink.h` | The shared coverage curve: gives kernel and ring-3 text dense stems while keeping smooth edges; light text uses contrast only. |
| `lib/libc.c` | `memcpy`, `memset`, `strlen` and the other handful of primitives a freestanding kernel cannot live without. |
| `third_party/bearssl/` | Vendored BearSSL subset (upstream 7bea48e, MIT): 90 flat `.c` files in `src/` for a minimal TLS 1.2 client (SSL client core and record layer, x509 minimal, i31 bignum, P-256, RSA/ECDSA verify, SHA-1/2, HMAC, HMAC_DRBG, constant-time AES, GCM, codec helpers), the full public header set in `inc/`, `src/inner.h`, `src/config.h`, and a one-line `string.h` shim onto `lib/libc.h`. The i386 kernel links only `src/sha2small.c`, `src/hmac.c`, `src/hmac_drbg.c`, `src/dec32be.c`, `src/enc32be.c` (entropy pool). Not yet wired into the Pi network code. File list: `tools/gen/bearssl-vendor.sh`; check: `tools/checks/bearssl-tls-check.py`. Nothing modified; see its README.md. |

#### Tasks and user programs

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `kernel/task.c` | Preemptive round-robin scheduling off the timer tick. Six slots. `yield()` reaches the same switch in software. |
| `kernel/ring3.c` + `kernel/ring3_asm.S` | Runs code at privilege level 3 with its own page tables, so a privileged instruction faults instead of taking the machine down. |
| `kernel/memmap.h` | The one place the fixed ring-3 addresses live (`JT_USER_BASE`, `JT_DMABUF_BASE`, `JT_USER_FB`); the Makefile renders it into `boot/memmap.ld` for the linker scripts. See the memory map paragraph below. |
| `arch/arm64/wifi.c`, `arch/arm64/wifi_proto.h`, `arch/arm64/wifi_fw_gen.sh` | M4 Wi-Fi, stage 1 (Pi 4's CYW43455 over SDIO; shape from Plan 9's ether4330.c and Circle's wlan). `wifi.c` sets GPIO 34-39 to ALT3, brings up the Arasan SDHCI, enumerates the SDIO card (CMD0/5/3/7, 4-bit, high speed, functions 1 and 2), loads the firmware and NVRAM into the chip's RAM over the backplane, releases the ARM, then talks BCDC over SDPCM: `ver`, `cur_etheraddr`, `clmload`, `country` CA and an `escan` whose results print as `wifi ap -51 ch6 Shaw`. Polled, every wait bounded, every step one short `wifi ...` line; QEMU powers the chip, finds no SDIO card and ends at `wifi FAIL cmd5`. `wifi_proto.h` is the pure logic (frame pack and parse, escan walk, NVRAM packing), tested on the host by `tools/checks/wifi-host-check.sh`. `wifi_fw_gen.sh` writes the gitignored wifi_fw.S (generated at build time, not in git) that embeds the Cypress firmware from `build/wifi-fw/` or empty stubs (`wifi no firmware`). |
| `arch/arm64/bt.c`, `arch/arm64/bt.h`, `arch/arm64/bt_fw_gen.sh` | Bluetooth, slice BT1 (2.51.0): the CYW43455's Bluetooth half over HCI in the H4 framing on the PL011 (GPIO 30-33, ALT3, hardware RTS/CTS), so the Pi image's console moved to the mini UART (`main.c`, `CONSOLE_MINI_UART`). Powers BT_ON (expander pin 128, through `wifi.c`'s `pi_exp_gpio`), then follows Linux's btbcm.c: Reset and Read Local Version at 115200, the BCM4345C0 patch (Download Minidriver, each `.hcd` record sent as the command it is, Launch RAM), Reset and version again, the vendor baud command to 921600, Set Event Mask with LE Meta on. `bt scan` (`cmd.c`) runs a 5 second active LE scan and lists each address once with its name (AD 0x09, else 0x08) and RSSI. Polled from the main loop, one command in flight, each with a 3 s deadline on the generic timer; a silent radio prints `bt: no answer from the radio`. `bt_fw_gen.sh` writes the gitignored `arch/arm64/bt_fw.S` that embeds `build/bt-fw/BCM4345C0.hcd` (or `BT_HCD=`), or nothing. On QEMU virt the radio is the second PL011 at 0x09040000, probed under `kprobe_armed`. Slice BT2 (2.54.0), `bt pair`: LE buffers (or the shared ones) and ACL flow control on Number of Completed Packets, Read BD_ADDR, LE Create Connection to the scanned mouse, SMP legacy Just Works on L2CAP channel 6 (c1 and s1 on BearSSL's aes_ct), LE Start Encryption with the STK, the mouse's LTK, EDIV and Rand kept (see BT3 below), then ATT on channel 4: the HID service, Protocol Mode written to boot, the Boot Mouse Input CCCD on. Each notification goes to `main.c`'s `kinput` as EV_REL and BTN_LEFT, like the USB mouse. The mouse's own ATT and signalling requests are answered. Stage lines `bt: connected`, `bt: paired`, `bt: mouse ready`; each wait has a 10 s deadline and a failure is one line and a hang-up. Slice BT3 (3.5.0), remembering: when the LTK, EDIV and Rand have all arrived the pairing is written to `JT/BTPAIR.BIN` (`bt: pairing saved`, never the key). At boot, once the radio is ready, a valid record skips scan and pairing: LE Create Connection to the saved address, LE Start Encryption with the saved key, `bt: reconnected`, then the same GATT steps. No answer in 12 s keeps the record and the next try follows after 2, 4, 8, 15 then 30 s (state RETRY, up to 30 tries; a dropped link starts the same run, `bt reconnect` restarts it, `bt status` reports it); Encryption Change 0x05 or 0x06, or a hang-up with 0x05, 0x06 or 0x3D during encryption, erases it. `bt forget` erases it; `bt` shows `saved pairing: yes` or `no`. |
| `arch/arm64/btpair.h` | The saved pairing's record, a 70 byte versioned file (magic `JTBP`, version, address type, address, LTK, Rand, EDIV, name, a 16 bit sum) as static inline pack and parse functions with no kernel calls, so the host check builds it with clang. A bad magic, another version, a short record, a damaged sum, an all zero key or a bad address type is refused. Its error words never carry the key. |
| `arch/arm64/emmc.c`, `arch/arm64/emmc.h` | M4 SD card (2.53.0 reads, 2.57.0 writes): the Pi 4 reads and writes the card it booted from. EMMC2 is the BCM2711's own SD host at 0xFE340000, on pins of its own, so there is no GPIO setup and the Wi-Fi host is never touched. Shape from Circle's lib/sdcard. Reset, bus power at 3.3 V, the base clock from the firmware (clock 12, `mbox_clock_rate` in `main.c`), 400 kHz, CMD0, CMD8, ACMD41 with HCS, CMD2, CMD3, CMD9 for the size, CMD7, CMD16 512, then 25 MHz on a 1-bit bus. Reads are polled PIO: CMD17 for one block, CMD18 with Auto CMD12 for more, refused past the card's end. Writes the same way back: CMD24 or CMD25 with Auto CMD12, then transfer done and CMD13 until the card has finished programming. `sd_mount` reads the MBR, takes the first FAT partition, checks its boot sector and FSInfo in one CMD18, and mounts it through `drivers/fat.c`, whose `blockdev_read_sector` and `blockdev_write_sector` it supplies (refused past the partition's end; a write never lands on the boot sectors, only FSInfo). `sd_write`, `sd_remove` and `sd_mkdir` keep the OS's files in a `JT` folder and refuse, with one `sd refused` line, any name that is not 8.3 or that the firmware boots from (config.txt, kernel*.img, start*.elf, any .dtb, the overlays folder and the rest). Every wait ends on the generic counter, and any failure prints one `sd ...` line (`sd no card`, `sd FAIL cmd8`) and the boot carries on. On virt (M3d, 2.58.0) only the four `emmc_*` calls differ: they go to `main.c`'s virtio disk (`vblk_*`, now with writes), so the same card mounts there through the same partition and `JT/` code; with no disk nothing is printed. `sd_enter_jt` is how `ufile.c` roots an app's paths. `tools/checks/arm64-sd-check.py` plugs a FAT32 card into QEMU's EMMC2, runs `ls` and `cat` through the `sdtest` build, then writes through `sdw` and reads back after a reboot through `sdr` (`make sdwtest`), and checks the card from the host with mtools, its own FAT pass and `fsck.fat`. |
| `arch/arm64/start.S`, `arch/arm64/vectors.S`, `arch/arm64/user.S`, `arch/arm64/main.c`, `arch/arm64/text.c`, `arch/arm64/libc.c`, `arch/arm64/linker.ld`, `arch/arm64/Makefile` | The ARM64 port (the ARM64 and the Raspberry Pi section), milestones M0, M1a, M1b, M1c, the first M2 drivers and M3a, the first userland. `vectors.S` is the exception table (a fault prints a line instead of hanging); `main.c` also sets up the interrupt controller and a timer tick, turns the MMU and caches on over a flat identity map, has a small bump heap, and gets a framebuffer (ramfb through fw_cfg on QEMU's virt machine, the VideoCore mailbox on a Pi) draws a first desktop into it and mirrors the boot log in its window (lines printed before the screen existed are replayed). The text is the same faces (Tree Sans since 3.0.0) through `drivers/ttf.c` as the i386 desktop: `text.c` blends each rasterized glyph onto the screen, `libc.c` supplies the few libc calls the rasterizer makes, and only those files use floating point (`start.S` turns the FPU on at EL1). The old bitmap font is the fallback if the faces fail to load, then on virt finds virtio devices among the virtio-mmio slots: a disk (it reads sector 0), a network card (it asks the router for its address over ARP) and a keyboard and tablet, read with one input driver. Devices raise their interrupt through the GIC and the main loop sleeps in `wfi` between events. A separate build from the i386 kernel: a boot stub that parks all but core 0, a linker script for QEMU's virt machine, and `main` printing over the PL011 UART. `make -C arch/arm64 run` boots it, `make -C arch/arm64 pi` makes the Raspberry Pi 4 image (`kernel8.img`, enters at EL2 and drops to EL1); `tools/checks/arm64-m0-check.py` proves both builds boot, and `tools/checks/arm64-m1c-check.py` takes a QEMU screendump and checks the pixels, and `tools/checks/arm64-console-scroll-check.py` presses Page Up, End and Home and checks the Console scrolls over the whole boot log, and `tools/checks/arm64-m2-check.py` presses keys, moves the pointer and clicks through QMP and reads them back. M3a: `user.S` is a small EL0 program the kernel copies into a user-only code page; `vectors.S` also takes `svc` and faults from EL0 and holds `enter_user` and `leave_user`; `main.c` splits the RAM map into 4 KiB pages for that one arena (code read-only, stack, and a page only the kernel may touch), answers `write` and `exit`, refuses to print memory the program does not own, and survives an EL0 fault as a printed line. `tools/checks/arm64-m3-check.py` asserts the exact serial lines. |
| `arch/arm64/ip.c`, `drivers/nic.h` | The IP stack on ARM. The ARM build compiles the i386 kernel's own `drivers/net.c` and `drivers/http.c` unchanged, with `drivers/nic.h` as the only seam to the hardware: init, MAC, send a frame, poll for one. `main.c` answers it with the virtio-net card on QEMU; the Pi's Wi-Fi or Ethernet goes in the same four calls. `ip.c` adds 100 Hz ticks off the generic timer and, at boot, leases an address by DHCP and (built with `NETPORT=`) POSTs to the host. `tools/checks/arm64-net-check.py` proves both. |
| `arch/arm64/rng.c`, `arch/arm64/rng.h` | Seeds TLS from the Pi 4 RNG200 hardware FIFO with health checks and a one-second deadline. Incomplete seeds are wiped and TLS stops; Pi images never link the timer provider used for QEMU checks. `tools/checks/arm64-rng-check.py` exercises register faults and byte bounds on the host. |
| `arch/arm64/clock_ui.h` | Read-only Clock window on ARM, opened through Spotlight or Samantha. Reuses Vancouver date conversion, refreshes each minute, and waits for network time. `tools/checks/arm64-calicon-check.py` covers its date, unset state, opening and closing. |
| `arch/arm64/settings.c` | The Pi's Settings choices and their file, 3.13.0. Wallpaper (grey by default `#6E6E6E`, terracotta, clay or ink, flat colours; the photo is gone since 3.0), dock tile size (5 to 25 percent, applied from the next boot) and the volume are saved as `volume=`, `wall=` and `dock=` lines in `JT/SETTINGS.TXT`, loaded right after the card mounts so the Pi's first desktop paint already has them. Every value is range-checked and a bad line is ignored; with no card the choices stay in memory and the window says so. `tools/checks/arm64-settings-check.py` runs the parser and the load and save paths on the host under sanitizers. |
| `arch/arm64/accounts.c` | User accounts on the Pi, 4.8.0 (slice 1 of 6.0 Yours; the login screen is `login.c`). Names and salted, iterated password hashes live in `JT/USERS.TXT`, one `name:p2:100000:saltHex:hashHex` line each, the same record `kernel/auth.h` writes: PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 from `kernel/auth_kdf.c` over BearSSL, a 16 byte salt from `tls_entropy`, a constant-time compare. Names are 1 to 24 of a-z 0-9 _ -, passwords 4 to 64, eight users at most. The file is untrusted: any bad line is skipped whole. A change is written first and kept in memory only if the card took it, so with no card an add is refused. The Terminal has `users`, `adduser NAME PASSWORD` and `deluser NAME PASSWORD`; `ask.c` stars the password out of the typed line and the UART. `tools/checks/arm64-accounts-check.py` runs it on the host under sanitizers, then in QEMU and across a reboot. |
| `arch/arm64/login.c`, `arch/arm64/login.h`, `arch/arm64/login_ui.h` | The Pi's login screen, 4.9.0 (slice 2 of 6.0). `login.c` is the form: a name (a-z 0-9 _ -, capitals folded) and a password in static buffers, Enter checks it with `accounts_check`, wipes the password and either sets the global `current_user` or waits 1000 ms (`login_wait_ms`) and sets a message that never says which half was wrong. `login_ui.h` (included by `main.c`) draws a cream card on the wallpaper colour with the mark, the name, two fields and the message, and while it is up (`login_live`) `input_event` hands it every key, drops the mouse, and the bar, notice and calendar painters stand back. With one or more accounts `login_show` runs at the end of boot; a right password calls `wall_repaint` and the name appears after the title in the menu bar. `logout` (cmd.c) and the tree menu's Log Out set a flag the main loop turns into: close every window, show the screen. No typed character is ever logged. `tools/checks/arm64-login-check.py` runs `login.c` on the host under sanitizers, then the screen in QEMU (screendumps, UART, logout, no-account boot) and a mutant that accepts any password. |
| `arch/arm64/sudo.c`, `arch/arm64/sudo.h` | Admins and sudo on the Pi, 4.10.0 (slice 3 of 6.0). `sudo.c` keeps the admin names in `JT/SUDOERS.TXT` (untrusted: a name that is no account is dropped, a card with accounts and no file makes the first account the admin), writes the card before memory for `makeadmin`, `unadmin` (never the last admin), the first account and the scrub of a removed name, and judges a sudo password in `sudo_check`: `accounts_check` on the signed-in name, `login_wait_ms` on a wrong one, the admin test, then the success is remembered for `sudo_ttl_ms` (300000, 12000 in the `sudoshort` test build) by the generic counter (`sudo_now_ms`). `cmd.c` gates the privileged commands through `priv()` (no accounts means open) and asks for a password through `ask_password` in `ask.c`: while it is up the bottom row shows the label, every key is a star, the echo is the label alone, the key lines stay off the UART (`ask_secret`), Esc cancels and the next Enter goes to `cmd_password`. `accounts.c` gained `accounts_passwd`, `accounts_delete`, `accounts_protected` (USERS.TXT and SUDOERS.TXT, refused to `write`, `rm`, Samantha's `[[save]]` and the apps' file calls in `ufile.c`) and a dummy PBKDF2 for an unknown name. `tools/checks/arm64-sudo-check.py` runs `sudo.c` on the host under sanitizers, then ann and bob in QEMU across three boots, the quiet-boot order and a mutant that skips the password check. |
| `tools/checks/fat-crash-check.py` | 4.13.0, slice 6 of 6.0: the real `fat.c` under sanitizers on FAT16 and FAT32 `mkfs.fat` images, power cut after every sector write of replace (more data, less data), delete, mkdir and rmdir. After each cut `fsck.fat -n` may report only unreachable clusters or unequal FAT copies; the mount scan repairs them, `fsck.fat -n` is then clean, and the file is exactly the old or the new content. A mutation that writes the entry before the data must fail, and so must one that switches the mount scan off. |
| `arch/arm64/home.c`, `arch/arm64/home.h` | Per-user home folders on the Pi, 4.11.0 (slice 4 of 6.0). `JT/HOME/<dir>` holds a signed-in person's files; `<dir>` is the account name up to 8 characters, else five letters, a tilde and two hex digits of a hash (adduser refuses a name whose folder name another account already has). `home_walk` is the one path walker the Terminal's `cat`, `ls`, `write`, `rm` and `mkdir` share: `~` expands to the folder, `.` and `..` parts are refused, and another person's folder is refused in any case unless sudo is active. `ufile.c` roots every app path at the folder (and remembers it in the descriptor, so a file open at sign-out flushes into its owner's folder); `ask.c`'s `[[save]]` writes `~/NAME`. `deluser` removes no files. Checked by `tools/checks/arm64-home-check.py` (host under sanitizers over an in-memory FAT, then QEMU, then two mutations). |
| `arch/arm64/users_ui.h` | The Users pane of Settings on ARM, 4.14.0, included by `settings_ui.h`. Views: the account list (with You and Admin marks and Add user), one account's page (Change password, an Admin switch, Remove user), a form (add user, change password), the admin password and the delete confirm. The work is the Terminal commands' own: `cmd.c` exports `users_add`, `users_delete` and `users_passwd`, which run the same `account_apply` and `passwd_apply` that `adduser`, `deluser` and `passwd` use, and the pane calls `sudo_make` and `sudo_unmake`. A privileged change asks the signed-in admin's password through `sudo_check` (the one place sudo is decided, so sudo's remembered five minutes apply); a normal user changes only their own password, with the old one; with no accounts Add user makes the first, an admin. Password fields are stars, buffers are wiped when the view changes, and `set_secret` keeps `key N` lines off the UART while one is up. Keys (arrows, Return, Esc) and clicks both work. `tools/checks/arm64-users-check.py` drives it in QEMU and catches a pane that skips the admin check. |
| `arch/arm64/settings_ui.h` | The Settings window on ARM, drawn in the Console's rectangle like the Clock. A sidebar of six sections (About, Display, Sound, Dock, Date and Time, Users) and a card of rows beside it, in the i386 Settings' layout. It opens from Spotlight, Samantha's `[[open settings]]` or a click on a menu bar status icon; arrows and the - and + buttons change a value and each change is saved at once. `tools/checks/arm64-settings-check.py` opens it, walks the sections, changes the wallpaper and the volume with keys and clicks, and reads the card back. |
| `arch/arm64/glyphs.h` | Flat anti-aliased glyphs drawn from circles, rounded boxes and capsules, 4x4 samples a pixel: the five Settings sidebar tiles and the menu bar's speaker and power symbol. No text, one ink. Covered by the same check. |
| `arch/arm64/clock.c` | Gives the Pi a clock from a verified HTTPS Date header, never plain HTTP. Its build time is the certificate bootstrap floor; accepted time cannot roll back. Strict bounded date parsing rejects malformed or duplicate headers and impossible dates. `tools/checks/arm64-clock-check.py` runs the parser and sync path with sanitizers on the host. The bootstrap age limit remains in `docs/GUIDE.md` (Encrypted Pi relay). |
| `arch/arm64/tls.c`, `arch/arm64/tls.h`, `arch/arm64/certs/` (37 public roots since 3.0.0: the browser said "tls error 62 (not trusted)" on example.com, whose chain ends at Comodo AAA, with only 7), `tools/gen/tls-ta.py`, `tools/gen/tls-roots.py` | HTTPS on the Pi. `tls.c` runs BearSSL (`third_party/bearssl`) over the stream TCP calls in `drivers/net.c` (`tcp_open`, `tcp_write`, `tcp_read`, `tcp_close`): TLS 1.2 only, AES-GCM and AES-CBC with ECDHE or RSA, every server certificate checked against the host name and the trust anchors baked in at build time. `tools/gen/tls-ta.py` turns the PEM roots in `certs/` (seven common ones, copied once by `tools/gen/tls-roots.py`) into the gitignored `arch/arm64/tls_ta.h`; `TLS_TA=extra.pem` adds one more, which is how the checks trust their throwaway server. Nothing is fetched at run time but the page. `https_fetch` returns the raw reply, `https_get` just the body. `tools/checks/arm64-tls-check.py` proves a page arrives with the anchor and is refused without it. |
| `arch/arm64/cmd.c` | The one command line on ARM. `cmd_run(line, n, sink)` takes a typed line and a function that prints, and dispatches it: `help`, the slash commands (`/model`, `/effort`, `/status`, `/clear`, `/help`, in `ask.c`), the browser (`browser.c`), `ls` and `cat NAME` on the SD card (`emmc.c`, `drivers/fat.c`), `llm PROMPT` and Claude (`ask.c`). Everything those print goes through `cmd_out`, so the Terminal's prompt calls it today and any other window can call it with its own sink. |
| `arch/arm64/audio.c`, `arch/arm64/audio.h` | Sound out of the Pi 4's 3.5 mm jack, polled for `beep`, through DMA for an app (M3e). Sets GPIO 40 and 41 to PWM1, runs the PWM clock at 27 MHz through the clock manager, and feeds both channels from the FIFO. `audio_play(samples, frames, rate)` takes interleaved 16-bit stereo; `audio_tone(freq, ms)` makes a quiet triangle wave. Samples pass through a 2048-frame ring and print `audio: N frames, ring sum 0x...`. Every wait is a counting loop that gives up with -1 and one line; the jack and GPIO 42 are never touched at boot. A build without `PI_BUILD` (QEMU virt) has no PWM and drains into nothing. A master volume (0 to 100, default 70, `audio_set_volume`) scales samples as they enter the ring; the line also prints `abs` (the sum of the samples' sizes) and the volume. Only `cmd.c` plays through it: `beep`, `vol` and `volume_key` (F11, F12 and the media keys from `main.c`'s `ui_key`). M3e (2.59.0), an app's sound for `JT_SYS_AUDIO` (`main.c` checks the pointers): `audio_app_queue` takes 8-bit unsigned mono into a 32 KB ring and never waits; `audio_pump`, called by the desktop's loop on every pass, counts what the hardware took, turns it back into silence, and moves the next bytes into a 16384-frame PWM ring as stereo words through the master volume, about an eighth of a second ahead. On the Pi a DMA channel (5, DREQ 1, PWM1's) loops over that ring into FIF1 by one control block that points at itself; on virt the generic counter takes it at the stream's rate. `played` is the app's frames the hardware took; the stream ends with one `audio: app stream N frames at R Hz, ring sum, abs, volume, played` line, and `beep` is refused while one runs. Unverified on a real board. Checks: `tools/checks/arm64-audio-check.py`, `tools/checks/arm64-volume-check.py`, `tools/checks/arm64-music-check.py` (its host part drives the Pi's DMA path against fake registers). |
| `arch/arm64/ask.c` (relay discovery), `tools/checks/arm64-relay-discovery-check.py` | The Pi finds the Claude relay by itself (2.46.0). It tries the relay that answered last, else the baked `CLAUDE_RELAY_HOST`; when that will not connect or will not prove itself, it broadcasts a 32-byte probe to the relay's port and tries the address the reply came from. `relay.py` answers whenever it runs TLS. The reply is untrusted: `tls_connect_private` checks the fixed name `jt-relay` against the `TLS_TA` anchors alone (`tls-ta.py --private`), never a public root, and the token goes out only after that handshake. The check boots with a dead baked address, puts a Python LAN on QEMU's network hub (user networking drops broadcasts) and wants an answer by probe, then a refusal for a right-CA certificate with the wrong name. |
| `arch/arm64/browser.c` | A text browser behind the one command line (`cmd.c`). `browse URL` (a bare host means https) fetches the page (https through `tls.c`, http through the shared stack), follows up to 3 redirects, turns the HTML into text (script and style skipped even when the code holds a `<`, entities and `&#NN;` decoded, a blank line before headings and paragraphs, `- ` list items, links as `text [3]`) and prints 16 lines at a time in 53-column rows, ending with a status line: the URL and `line X of Y`. `more`, `up`, `down`, `top`, `bottom` move, `find WORD` jumps to a line, `links` lists the numbered URLs, `open N` follows one, `back` and `forward` walk the last 8 pages, `reload` fetches again. Every failure is one line: dns, connect, tls, http status, page too big, too many redirects. `tools/checks/arm64-browser-check.py` proves all of it against a local HTTPS server. A fetch answers or fails in about 10 s and prints `dns ok`, `tcp ok`, `tls ok`, `http NNN` as it goes; `tools/checks/arm64-browser-deadserver-check.py` proves the timeouts. |
| `arch/arm64/mascot_art.h`, `tools/gen/gen_mascot_art.py`, `tools/gen/mascot.py` | The mascot on the Pi: `mascot.py` draws it (the logo's crayon hand on a chubby tree with a face, `landing/mascot.svg`), `gen_mascot_art.py` rasterizes it without grain to a 192 px RGBA PNG and writes it as `mascot_art.h`, and the Browser (`browser_ui.h`) decodes it with the kernel's own `png_decode` and draws it, 2x2 samples a pixel, on its empty well above "Nothing to show yet". |
| `arch/arm64/browser_ui.h`, `tools/checks/arm64-browser-app-check.py` | The Browser window (2.66.0), opened from Spotlight, in the Console's rectangle like Clock and Brick. A toolbar with Back, Forward, Reload and an address bar you type into (Enter loads, Esc cancels), the page as text in Tree Sans wrapped to the window, link lines in the accent colour that a click follows, Page Up, Page Down, Home, End and the wheel with the scroll clamped, and one status line (URL and line X of Y, `loading...`, or a plain-word error). It runs the Terminal's own `browse`, `open N`, `back`, `forward` and `reload` with `cmd_out` pointed at a sink, and reads the page through `browser.c`'s accessors (`browser_text`, `browser_url`, `browser_link`, `browser_loads`). Alt+Left, Alt+Right, F5 and Ctrl+R navigate, Ctrl+L focuses the address. Bookmarks (3.9.9): a star at the bar's right end, or Ctrl+D, adds the open page to `JT/BOOKMARK.TXT` (`address title` a line, 12 at most, read once on the first open, written whole through `sd_write` on each change; the name is 8.3, so not BOOKMARKS) or takes it off when it is there; with no page open the well lists them in place of the mascot, Tab picks and Enter or a click opens one (`browser_title` is the new accessor). Check: `tools/checks/arm64-bookmarks-check.py`. UART lines `browser: load`, `link N`, `back`, `page N lines`. Check: a stub server, QEMU keys and clicks, serial lines and screendump pixels. |
| `user/libjt/hires.h` | Sharp 2x drawing for ring-3 apps, header-only (3.9.0). An app opens its window with `jt_window_open_hires`; on the ARM64 kernel at scale 2 the buffer is device size (twice the width and height, pitch in bytes twice `width * 4`) while `width` and `height` stay logical, so the app lays out as before. `jt_scale` reads the 2 off the pitch (a scratch struct with a made-up pitch, or any other kernel, reads 1), and `jt_fill`, `jt_fill_dev`, `jt_pixel` and `jt_disc` draw in logical pixels at either scale: a rectangle edge is hard, a circle is antialiased with 4 by 4 samples a device pixel and has the 1x footprint. Text goes through `JT_SYS_TEXT2` (402, ARM64 only): `text.c` and `mono.c` send each run with the 1x atlas's own advances times two, and `main.c`'s `app_text` rasterises each glyph with Tree Sans (the Console's rasterizer) at twice the size straight into the buffer, so a line is exactly as wide as at 1x and Notes' caret and Calendar's centring do not move. `app_blit` copies the buffer 1 to 1; an app that does not ask keeps the 1x buffer and the bilinear blit. Done for Calendar, Notes, Music and Books (and the mascot and the phone keyboard they link); the kernel-drawn windows already draw at device size. |
| `user/libjt/mascot.h`, `tools/checks/arm64-mascot-empty-check.py` | The mascot in a ring-3 app window, ARM64 build only (3.7.0). `jt_mascot_draw` decodes `arch/arm64/mascot_art.h` once with libjt's `png_decode` and blends it, 2x2 samples a pixel, over the window; `user/music.c` and `user/books.c` call it behind `__aarch64__` above "No songs yet" and "No books yet", so the i386 apps are unchanged. Check: boots both apps on a card with no songs or books and finds the mascot's ink and terracotta in a screendump, failing if the draw is reverted. |
| `arch/arm64/ask.c` (sam speed), `arch/arm64/tls.c`, `tools/checks/arm64-samfast-check.py` | `sam` is fast on the board (3.7.6). Plain arithmetic (digits, `+ - * / ^`, brackets, decimals, quotes and a "what is" around it) is answered in `sam_command` by `calc_eval_str`, the Calculator's own evaluator in `calc.c`, so there is no second parser, and nothing is queued or sent. For the relay, `relay_open` tries the address in memory, then `JT/RELAY.TXT` on the card (`a.b.c.d:port`, written only when the address changes, no token), each with a 2 s connect, and only then the baked host (3 s) and the LAN probe; the handshake still has to prove the name `jt-relay`, so a wrong hint cannot get the token. The request asks for one or two sentences through the `pi` field, which the relay puts in Samantha's system text, so `relay.py` is unchanged. The reply wait is 30 s (`ASK_TICKS`): then `sam: no answer in 30s (relay at a.b.c.d)` and the busy flag clears. `tls_exchange` stops reading once Content-Length bytes are in instead of waiting for the server to close. Every question ends with `sam: N.Ns (find a, tls b, wait c)` from `ticks()`. The check runs a real relay on a Python LAN: sums with no traffic, the second question with 0 probes, a TLS server that never answers, and a reboot that reads `RELAY.TXT` from a virtio card. |
| `arch/arm64/ask.c`, `arch/arm64/claude_cfg.sh` | Claude in the ARM Terminal. `ask.c` is a one-line editor on the Terminal's bottom row (the zsh style prompt `joshua@tree % `, or `NAME@tree % ` when signed in, Backspace, Enter) that hands the line to `cmd.c`'s `cmd_run`; `sam QUESTION` queues a question that `ask_poll` posts to the Claude relay through `drivers/http.c` on a later main loop pass, keeping the relay's session for follow-ups. While `sam` waits, `drivers/net.c` calls `net_idle` in every client wait loop and `ask.c` uses it to poll the keyboard and run lines typed meanwhile, so the prompt never freezes; an unknown word is `zsh: command not found` in `cmd.c`, and the answer prints as `sam: ...`. Everything a command prints goes into the Terminal, wrapped to 53 columns. Every outcome is one line: thinking, an HTTP error code, timeout, no network, no token. If the image has a local model (`llm.c`), its answer follows that line. `claude_cfg.sh` writes the relay's host, port and token into a gitignored header at build time, from environment variables and a token file outside the repo, only when `JT_WIFI_DEV=1` (dev cards) or `CLAUDE_RELAY_TOKEN_FILE` is set (the checks); a release build carries no token and no relay host, and the token is never printed. `tools/checks/arm64-token-gate-check.py` proves the gate and a release build has none. `tools/checks/arm64-claude-console-check.py` proves it and `tools/checks/arm64-zsh-check.py` proves the prompt stays usable while she thinks. |
| `arch/arm64/main.c` (Console and Terminal) | The Console and two switchable Terminal sessions share one log renderer, each with its own scrollback: the Console (boot log, Wi-Fi, errors; no input) and the Terminal (the command line). The Terminal dock tile, F2 or Ctrl+T brings the Terminal up in the Console's place; Esc goes back. With no pane holding the keys, F1, Cmd, Ctrl or Alt+Space, or any letter, opens Spotlight (`ui_key`). A dev build (`KEY_DEBUG`, `JT_WIFI_DEV=1`) shows the last raw HID report and key name in the menu bar for 3 seconds. Only the Terminal takes typing, and what a command prints lands there. F3 switches sessions when idle; each preserves its typed line, AI conversation, model, effort and status counters. The UART stream is the same either way. See `docs/GUIDE.md` (Terminal). |
| `arch/arm64/ask.c` (agent loop), `docs/GUIDE.md` (Samantha's agent loop on the Pi), `tools/checks/arm64-agent-check.py`, `tools/checks/pi-release-notoken-check.sh` | Samantha as an agent on the Pi. An answer may end with actions, one per line, 80 characters each, 4 per turn: `[[note TEXT]]`, `[[say TEXT]]`, `[[led blink]]`, `[[open APP]]`, `[[browse URL]]` (http or https only, through `browser.c`), `[[calc EXPR]]` (`calc.c`), `[[status]]`. The Pi runs them, prints each outcome, and posts the outcomes back to the relay as the next turn in the same session, up to 5 steps, with an `agent: step N` line each; unknown actions print `agent: ignored [[...]]` and do nothing. Esc or the word `stop` ends the loop between steps. In `--api-key-file` mode the relay keeps a short per-session history so the chain has context, and its `SAMANTHA` prompt lists the actions and the rule to ask before anything that would change a file. A Pi image gets the relay token only with `JT_WIFI_DEV=1` (the dev card); a release image has none. `tools/checks/pi-actions-check.py` proves the parser on the host, `arm64-agent-check.py` the loop under QEMU against a fake relay, and `pi-release-notoken-check.sh` the release rule. The Terminal also takes `/model`, `/effort`, `/status`, `/clear` and `/help` on the Pi (`slash` in `ask.c`; the prompt then shows the model and, when not the default, the effort), proved by `tools/checks/arm64-model-check.py`; the relay checks the two words in `tools/checks/claude-relay-check.py`. |
| `arch/arm64/llm.c`, `arch/arm64/llm_model.S` | A small language model in the ARM kernel, no network. `llm.c` is the llama2.c forward pass (float32, greedy) with its tokenizer, using llama2.c's own checkpoint and tokenizer files. `llm_model.S` bakes them into the image when the build is given `LLM_MODEL=` and `LLM_TOK=`; on a Pi the firmware then loads them from the SD card with the kernel. Without them `llm` says there is no model. `llm PROMPT` at the Terminal's prompt prints the text and tokens per second. The relay stays the default: only a question it cannot take (no token, no network, no answer) goes to the local model, and only if the image has one. `tools/checks/arm64-llm-check.py` compares the kernel's text with its own Python copy of the forward pass. |
| `tools/checks/arm64-claude-console-check.py` | Boots the ARM build on QEMU virt with virtio-net, ramfb and a virtio keyboard, runs the real relay on 127.0.0.1 with a stub `claude` and a throwaway token, proves typing with the Console in front asks nothing, opens the Terminal with F1, types a question with QMP keys and Enter, and asserts the stub got the exact text and its answer comes back in lines of at most 53 columns, drawn in the Terminal and not in the Console. Also a session resume, a wrong token (`claude: error -401`), no network card (`claude: no network`) and a build with no token (`claude: no token`). Skips where clang, lld or qemu is missing. |
| `arch/arm64/main.c` (dock) | The ARM desktop's dock (2.22.0) is the i386 dock: the same tray, shadows, icon artwork and layout from `kernel/gui_paint.c` and `kernel/dock_geom.c`, drawn through a small window shim over the framebuffer (scale 2 at 1080p, 1 below). Each icon's PNG is decoded and scaled between `heap_mark` and `heap_release`. Since 2.23.0 it also has the i386 hover label (`dock_hover`, which keeps a copy of the dock band so a label can be taken away again) and the Console wears the i386 window frame; the painters' text goes through `text.c`. Since 2.24.0 the pointer drives it: a USB or virtio mouse moves the i386 arrow (saved and put back around every other drawing that touches it, and cleaned out of the cache), the label follows the slot under it, the Console's red button closes the window and a dock click reopens it. The `hovertest` and `cursortest` builds show the label and the arrow at boot. No live Clock face yet; Calendar is plain art, the same picture on both builds. `tools/checks/arm64-m1c-check.py` checks the tray, hairline, all 11 tiles, the window frame and the hover label at 800x600 and 1080p. |
| `tools/checks/arm64-mouse-check.py` | Boots the ARM build on QEMU virt with ramfb and a USB keyboard and mouse behind a hub, moves the mouse through QMP and checks screendumps: the arrow at the pointer, the hover label over a dock slot and gone again with the dock's band back pixel for pixel, the close button, a dock click that reopens the Console, the Terminal tile opening the Terminal with its prompt, and a mouse plugged in after boot. Then the `cursortest` build at 1080p on the Pi 4B model for the scale-2 arrow. |
| `tools/checks/arm64-focus-check.py` | Boots the ARM build on QEMU virt with a USB keyboard and mouse behind a hub, opens the Terminal and then the Calculator, and types into each after clicking it. The UART and screendumps must show the sum in the Calculator, the digits on the Terminal's prompt row only, the clicked window drawn on top, and the boot wallpaper back when the Calculator closes. |
| `tools/checks/arm64-clipboard-check.py` | 3.10.0: boots the ARM kernel headless on QEMU virt with a FAT32 card on virtio and the keyboard's Super key as Cmd. Notes opens from Spotlight, a note is typed, Cmd+A selects it (`edsel=0,7`), Cmd+C copies (`CLIPCOPY:7`); the Terminal's Cmd+V pastes (`CLIPPASTE:7`) and Enter runs the line, which the shell can only answer with `zsh: command not found: pasteok`. Cmd+X cuts a typed line and Cmd+V returns it. Cmd+W closes the Terminal, then Notes the way its red dot does. The clipboard itself is `clip_put`/`clip_take` in `arch/arm64/main.c`: one 4 KB buffer, `JT_SYS_CLIPBOARD` (399) for ring-3 apps, direct calls from `ask.c` (`ask_edit`, the input line) and `browser_ui.h` (the address bar). |
| `arch/arm64/main.c` (window drag) | 3.6.0: a press on the title band of the Console, Terminal, Clock, Brick or Browser (their one shared rectangle, `win_lx`, `win_ly`) starts a drag, the button going up ends it and the UART says `window at X,Y`. `win_move_to` is the one place that moves them: it clamps the window whole on screen below the menu bar and above the dock's band, copies the pixels (nothing is redrawn, so the log and the page come along), fills the uncovered strips with the flat wallpaper, shifts the Console's text origin (`con_x`, `con_y`, `con_wx`, `con_wy`) by the same amount and cleans both rectangles. The Calculator and app windows keep their boot places, and the Console is not dragged while one is open. Closing a window fills the wallpaper at its current place (`wall_fill`), so no saved copy of the desktop is kept. |
| `tools/checks/arm64-drag-check.py` | Boots the ARM build on QEMU virt with a USB keyboard and mouse behind a hub, presses and moves through QMP and checks screendumps: a press in the well drags nothing, a title-band drag moves the dots, leaves flat wallpaper, keeps the window's pixels and the dock band, the clamps at the right and bottom and at the left and menu bar, the red dot closing the moved Console, the Browser opening where it was put and dragging the same way. Then the `dragtest` build at 1080p on the Pi 4B model for the scale-2 drag. |
| `tools/checks/arm64-appsize-check.py`, `tools/checks/arm64-sharp2x-check.py` | `appsize` builds the `apptest` kernel for Calendar, Notes, Music and Books in turn (the app opened at boot), boots each on the Pi 4B model at 1080p and checks the window's frame stays near the Console's size and its content is not blank. `sharp2x` (3.9.0) does the same boots and reads each screendump for crisp text: it finds every narrow ink run in the content (a text stem), and a stem must have at most two soft pixels (a 1x picture smoothed up has three or four) and two or more full ones, over at least 100 stems (40 for the rest). It covers every ARM window: the four EL0 apps plus the Calculator, Clock, Brick, Browser, Terminal and Console, which the `apptest` build opens by name and reports on the UART (`WINBOX`); the kernel-drawn ones score 97 to 99 percent, as they already drew at device size. Settings, Trash and Samantha have no window on the ARM build yet (their tiles open the Console). Before 3.9.0 it fails on the same four screens (a stem averaged 3.7 to 4.2 soft pixels), after it passes (1.1 to 1.3). `--shots DIR` keeps the screendumps as PPM and PNG. |
| `tools/checks/arm64-browser-flicker-check.py`, `arch/arm64/main.c` (off_begin, off_end) | 3.6.1: the Browser no longer flickers on the Pi, where the framebuffer is the live scanout. `browser_paint` copies the window's rectangle into one window-sized heap block (2.3 MB at 1080p, taken once at boot, like the dock band copy, so no heap_mark release can rewind over it), points `fb` at it shifted so screen coordinates still work, draws the whole window there, then `off_end` copies it back row by row, top to bottom, and cleans the cache once. If the heap cannot give the block it paints in place as before. Check: the `brtrace` build (`-DBR_TRACE`) hashes the real screen's window rectangle before a paint and just before the copy and logs whether it changed; open, load, Tab, scroll and address typing must each log offscreen with 0, never `paint direct`. |
| `tools/checks/arm64-browser-mouse-check.py`, `arch/arm64/browser_ui.h` (br_wrap, browser_wheel) | 3.7.2: pointer motion over a long Browser page. `pointer_moved` only swaps the arrow (save the pixels under it, restore, draw) and the dock label, and never calls `browser_paint`; the check proves it on the `brtrace` build by counting `browser: paint` and `browser: wrap` lines across 120 motion events, and the screen equals the reference afterwards. `br_wrap` keeps the lines it made while the page text, its load count and the well width are unchanged (a scroll or reopen does not measure every character again), and wheel notches are summed per report and ignored when the page is already at that end, so a stray wheel byte cannot repaint the page. |

| `tools/checks/arm64-splash-check.py`, `arch/arm64/main.c` (splash_mark) | 3.8.3: the Pi boot splash is neutral grey `0xE5E5E7` with no terracotta, the tree mark in ink `0x1C1C1E` and a grey bar (track `0xC8C8CC`, done `0x636366`). The old splash drew the 80 px menu bar copy of the mark stretched 2.75x over orange, which read as a dark smudge on the Pi; `splash_mark` samples the 160 px `boot_mark_cov` and writes ink over the known ground straight to the screen, then `fb_flush`es the box and the ground (unflushed rows reached the real Pi as torn slivers). The ARM wallpaper is graphite `0x6E6E6E`. The check also compares the mark row by row against `boot_mark_cov`. It boots the splash build headless, screendumps while it holds and asserts the grey ground, zero terracotta pixels, many ink pixels in the mark's box and hard edges. |

| `arch/arm64/main.c` (quiet boot, Wi-Fi fan), `tools/checks/arm64-quietboot-check.py` | 3.7.10: the splash never hides a failure. `uart_puts` flags any `FAIL` or `oom ` line printed while `splash_on` and `uart_putc` calls `splash_abort` at the end of that line: the splash ends, `fb_desktop` paints (`M1c fb ok`), and `splash_finish` later prints `boot done` and `fb_diag` only. `splash ` lines stay off the screen log, so a crash report's last console lines are real boot lines. A crash while the splash is up draws the red panel over the terracotta (`arm64-crash-check.py` on the Pi image: `splash up`, then the report, and a terracotta pixel beside the panel). The Pi checks that waited for `M1c fb ok` before Wi-Fi lines (`arm64-sd-check.py`, crash) now wait for `splash up` and the desktop after the first FAIL. 3.7.4: the Pi image boots behind a splash and draws no Console window at all (`QUIET_BOOT`, on with `PI_BUILD`). `fb_init` paints only `splash_begin`: solid terracotta `0xB5502C`, the tree mark (`menu_mark_paint`, the one the menu bar and the old boot screen use) centred and a thin track under it that `splash_step` fills one sixth at a time for fb, sd, wifi, clock, bt and desktop (UART: `splash up`, `splash step N name`). `main` calls the steps as `sd_mount`, the Wi-Fi join and its clock sync and `bt_init` finish, waits up to 60 s while `bt_starting` (the radio loading its patch), then `splash_finish` prints `boot done` and `fb_desktop` paints the wallpaper, menu bar and dock once. `menubar_wifi` keeps its state meanwhile. `-DSPLASH_STEP_MS=N` holds each step for the check. 3.6.2: the Pi image draws no Console window at boot (`QUIET_BOOT`, on with `PI_BUILD`). `fb_init` skips `console_frame` and `con_start`, so the boot log stays in `con_p` and the UART prints `console quiet`; `pane_open` draws it whole when a dock click or Esc from the Terminal asks. The old close-after-boot step, which showed the window for the length of the Wi-Fi bring-up and kept it on any FAIL line, is gone. The menu bar's Wi-Fi fan is anti-aliased, flat ink `0x001C1C1E` when joined and neutral grey `0x909090` otherwise, never blue. The check boots virt with `-DQUIET_BOOT` and `-DWIFI_ICON_TEST` (which prints where the fan is, and `=2` forces a joined one): flat wallpaper where the window would be, the fan's pixels, a dock click opening the Console with the buffered log, the Terminal tile opening the Terminal. |
| `arch/arm64/calc.c` | The Calculator on ARM. The parser is `user/calculator.c`'s, with two changes: dividing by zero is an error, and `30deg` marks an angle in degrees. Standard and scientific keypads, degrees or radians, `ans` and memory keys. The math runs as short series on the FPU, with no libm. `main.c` draws it as its own window over the right of the Console's, so both show: the Apps tile (by click, dock keys or Spotlight) opens it, and you click keys or type. The window in front has the keys; a click on the other brings it up (`tools/checks/arm64-focus-check.py`). `tools/checks/arm64-calc-check.py` checks 42 sums on the host, then boots the `calctest` build and reads the same answers off the UART. |
| `tools/checks/arm64-keys-check.py` | Boots the ARM build on QEMU virt with a virtio keyboard and no pointer, and drives `main.c`'s keyboard-only desktop through QMP: Cmd+Space and F1 open Spotlight (its off-white bar and accent mark in a screendump), typing `term` and Enter opens the Terminal and a typed question reaches ask.c, Esc hands the keys back, Left and Right move the dock's label and Enter opens that tile, F2 opens the Terminal at once. |
| `tools/checks/arm64-keydbg-check.py` | Mac-style USB keyboards, over QEMU's xHCI and usb-kbd: boots `keydbg-kernel8.elf` (`make keydbgtest`), whose made-up HID reports prove `xhci.c`'s `hid_kbd` reads past a report ID; Cmd+Space opens Spotlight and the dev-only key debug line shows `08 00 2c ...` and `Cmd+Space`; a plain letter on the bare desktop opens Spotlight with it typed; Esc with it closed does nothing. The release `kernel8.elf` has no debug line. |
| `tools/checks/arm64-calicon-check.py` | Builds the `caltest` variant twice with the clock fixed at two dates, boots each headless and reads the Calendar tile out of a screendump: the accent pixels all sit in the cell that date names in a seven-column month grid, with grey day squares around it. It also opens the Calendar app at 23:20 Vancouver time (already past midnight UTC) and finds the circled day, the local one; `--mutate` puts the grid back on UTC and must fail. |
| `tools/checks/arm64-calc-check.py` | Compiles `arch/arm64/calc.c` on the host and checks a table of sums, including sin(30deg)=0.5, 5!=120, 2^10=1024 and 1/0 as an error. It also checks the keypad's memory, Deg and edit keys. Then it boots the `calctest` build headless and checks the UART and a screendump. |
| `arch/arm64/wifi.c` (`wifi_hold`, `wifi_release`) | Bluetooth pairing holds the Wi-Fi radio (3.0.0): the chip has one antenna, and with Wi-Fi up a real mouse's first packets never reached the Pi (0x3e at every link speed on the board, 2026-10-12; with Wi-Fi off the same mouse paired first try). `bt pair` calls `wifi_hold` (WLC_DOWN, the menu bar shows Wi-Fi working) and every end of pairing, paired or failed, calls `wifi_release` (WLC_UP and the join again). |
| `arch/arm64/wall.c` | The ARM desktop's wallpaper: one flat colour, the brand terracotta `#b5502c` in 3.0.0 and graphite grey `#6E6E6E` by default since 3.9.0 (2.15.0 to 2.64 decoded the Satellite photo here). `tools/checks/arm64-m1c-check.py` checks the wallpaper pixels at 800x600 and 1080p. |
| `arch/arm64/userapps.S`, `arch/arm64/user_c.ld` | M3b (2.49.0) and M3c part 1 (2.50.0), C programs at EL0. The Makefile builds `user/hello.c`, `user/calendar.c` and, since M3d (2.58.0), `user/notes.c`, and since M3e (2.59.0) `user/music.c`, unchanged for aarch64 (`-fPIE -fdirect-access-external-data`, plus libjt's `string.c`, `text.c`, `osk.c`, `stdlib.c`, `wav.c` and `mp3.c` under `ujt_*.o` names, never in `user/libjt/`; `mp3.c` alone without `-mgeneral-regs-only`, since minimp3 works in floating point) and links each `-pie` at 0 with `user_c.ld` into a flat image: code and read-only data, then a page-aligned data half, then a 16 byte trailer (`JTU1`, code length, `.rela.dyn` range) and `userapps.S` bakes the images in with `.incbin` and a `{name, start, end}` table. `main.c`'s `user_load` copies one into the app window (0x10000 into the user arena), applies its `R_AARCH64_RELATIVE` entries to the copy (any other kind refuses the load), maps the code pages read-only and executable, the data pages and 32 KiB of zeroed pool read-write and never executable (Notes keeps a 28 KB arena there), and a sixteen page stack (four before M3e) with nothing mapped below it. `user_in_range` covers exactly that window, the stack and, since M3e, the heap below the app's `JT_SYS_BRK` top. `fpkeep` (M3e) is a hand-made image like `loop` that holds q0-q31, FPCR and FPSR across 40 yields and calls. `exc_el0_sync` answers `JT_SYS_WRITE` (fd 1 and 2), `JT_SYS_EXIT`, `JT_SYS_TIME` (the ARM clock, 0 if unset; stores only into writable user memory), `JT_SYS_GETPID`, since M3c part 2 the window calls and `JT_SYS_SCHED_YIELD` (next row), and since M3d the file calls (`ufile.c`); anything else is -ENOSYS, and every call but write, read, exit, yield, poll and (M3e) audio prints a `M3c svc N` line on the UART. The boot self-test (QEMU build) runs hello, then `user.S`'s entry E, which abuses each file call once (a kernel pointer, a read into the kernel page, a path with `..`, a 4 GB readfile, a readdir past its page: each an errno), then calendar, then `user.S`'s probe against calendar's pages twice with a reload between, then branches into data, pool and stack. The `userrun NAME` command (`cmd.c`) runs an app from the Terminal on QEMU and the Pi. `tools/checks/arm64-m3c-check.py` asserts all of it; `arm64-m3-check.py` still asserts hello |
| `arch/arm64/ufile.c` | M3d (2.58.0): the file calls of `user/jtsys.h` for the one EL0 app, with i386's semantics from the System call ABI section: `open` (the access modes, `O_CREAT`, `O_TRUNC`, `O_APPEND`, anything else -EINVAL; a file over 8192 bytes -EINVAL), `read` and `write` on fds 3 to 7 (255 bytes a call, the buffer checked before the fd, -EFBIG past 8192), `close` (writes the buffer back with `fat_replace_file`, reads it back and compares, -EIO when they differ), `lseek` (-ESPIPE on 0 to 2, -EINVAL past the end), `readdir` (40-byte records, the same on LP64; the whole array checked first; the count can exceed what was asked), `readfile` (up to 6 MB into the caller's buffer), `mkdir` (-ENOSPC) and `unlink` (-EISDIR on a folder). Paths are relative to the card's `JT/` folder, the apps' root: no leading slash, no `.` or `..`, at most 63 bytes and 8 parts, copied a byte at a time through `main.c`'s `user_in_range`; outputs go through `user_writable`, never into code. No card is -ENOENT. Five static 8 KB buffers, since the ARM heap never frees; `ufile_release` closes and flushes whatever an app left open when it exits, faults or is killed. It runs over `drivers/fat.c` on the partition `emmc.c` mounted (the Pi's SD card, or the virtio disk on virt) and puts fat.c's one current directory back (`fat_cwd_get`, `fat_cwd_set`) before it returns, so the Terminal's `ls` and `cat` still start at the root. That is only safe with one app, parked only at a yield. `tools/checks/arm64-m3c-check.py` (the abuse pass, and hello.c to exit 9 on a virtio card) and `tools/checks/arm64-notes-check.py` (Notes saves and reopens a note across a reboot on raspi4b) prove it. |
| `arch/arm64/main.c` (EL0 apps in windows), `arch/arm64/vectors.S` (`resume_user`) | M3e (2.59.0): the EL0 arena is 16 MiB, eight 2 MiB blocks of 4 KiB pages; blocks two to eight are the app's `JT_SYS_BRK` heap (`app_brk_set`: 0 reads the top, any other value sets it to the byte, pages mapped EL0 read-write and never executable and zeroed, shrinking unmaps, outside the heap -EINVAL), dropped with the app. `JT_SYS_AUDIO` is answered through `audio.c` (`app_audio`), `JT_SYS_AUDIO_RECORD` is -ENOSYS, and a call that repeats the last `M3c svc` line exactly is not logged again. `el0_sync_entry` keeps an app's q0-q31, FPCR and FPSR around every call; `app_park` copies them out and `resume_user` loads them back, `enter_user` starts a program with them zero, and the kernel's own d8-d15 are kept with x19-x30. Music is a Spotlight entry (`SPOT_MUSIC`), not a dock tile, and `app_start` makes `JT/MUSIC` for it. M3c part 2 (2.56.0): one app at a time under a cooperative scheduler, the i386 model. The dock (a tile named after an app in the table: Calendar, and since 2.58.0 Notes, for which `app_start` first makes `JT/NOTES/NOTES`, its default folder), Spotlight and `userrun` call `app_start`; the desktop's loop calls `app_tick`, which resumes the app with `resume_user` (the kernel's state saved as `enter_user` saves it, then every user register, the pc and `sp_el0` put back) until it yields. `JT_SYS_SCHED_YIELD` parks it (`app_park`: the frame copied out, `leave_user`), so the loop goes on with the keys, the mouse and the clock and runs it again on the next pass with an event, or every 10 ms. `JT_SYS_WINDOW_OPEN` (only under the scheduler; the boot self-test gets -ENODEV) maps a buffer of the window's content size at the top of the arena (0xD00000 since 3.9.0, 3 MiB, so a device-size buffer fits; it was 0x90000, and the app's heap now ends where the buffer starts, 11 MiB), EL0 read-write and never executable, and draws the Calculator's frame titled with the app's name over the right of the Console, as tall as fits above the dock (560x360 at 800x600); the LP64 `jt_window_info` has the pixels pointer at +16. `JT_SYS_WINDOW_POLL` copies the buffer to the screen on `JT_POLL_PRESENT` (each pixel scale by scale, only while the window is in front and Spotlight is not over it), then returns one queued event (12-byte LP64 `jt_event`) or -EAGAIN. The window in front gets the keys as `JT_KEY_*` codes and clicks in content coordinates; a click on it when behind raises it; its red dot is i386's close (a click above the content, which calendar answers by exiting; an app that ignores it is ended a quarter second later, `app_kill`), and an exit, a fault or a kill puts the wallpaper saved at boot back and redraws the Console. Opening it closes the Calculator; the Calculator, Brick or the Clock end it. `userapps.S`'s `loop`, a hand-made image that opens a window and yields forever, is the test of that. A program that never makes a syscall still holds the core: preempting it needs IRQs at EL0. `tools/checks/arm64-calendar-check.py` proves it. |
| `arch/arm64/pci.c`, `arch/arm64/xhci.c` | M4, USB keyboard and mouse on ARM. `pci.c` walks PCI Express (ECAM on QEMU's virt machine, the BCM2711 root complex on a Pi 4, which it also brings up before asking the firmware to load the VL805's own firmware), numbers bridges, gives memory BARs an address and finds the xHCI controller. `xhci.c` drives it polled: reset, device table, command and event rings, then enables, addresses and configures every device on the root ports and behind hubs (route strings, plus the transaction translator a slow keyboard needs behind the Pi's high-speed hub). Boot-protocol keyboards and mice become Linux input events for `main.c`, and each key is echoed on screen. Every step prints a short `usb ...` line, so a photo of the screen shows how far a real board got. `tools/checks/arm64-usb-check.py` proves it under QEMU with the controller behind a root port and the keyboard behind a hub. A boot keyboard report with a report ID in front (9 bytes) is read past the ID; a keyboard's second HID interface (a Mac-mode top row) is opened too, and its brightness and Spotlight media keys become F1 and F2. Every keyboard report also goes to `main.c`'s `key_raw`, the dev card's key debug line. |
| `arch/arm64/xhci.c` (USB audio) | A USB microphone or speaker works through isochronous transfers, one a millisecond. The driver reads the device's descriptors, picks a 16-bit 48 or 44.1 kHz setting, and keeps 120 transfers queued. A microphone is mixed down to 16 kHz mono and kept only while `mic` (cmd.c) is listening. `tools/checks/arm64-usbaudio-check.py` streams a tone to QEMU's USB speaker and reads it back from a WAV. QEMU has no microphone, so the microphone direction needs the real Pi. |
| `tools/claude-relay/stt.py` | Speech to text on the Mac, a small HTTP service beside relay.py (which it never touches): raw 16 kHz mono PCM in with the relay's bearer token, whisper.cpp runs the base English model, the words come back as JSON. `tools/checks/stt-check.py` speaks a sentence with macOS `say` and wants it back. |
| `arch/arm64/ask.c` (`hear_post`) | Speech into Samantha on the Pi: the samples voice mode recorded go raw (16 kHz) to the words service one port above the relay (the same token, and on the Pi the same TLS name); the words are printed as `you: ...` and queued as a `sam` question. Since 4.19.0 there is no typed `hear` command: holding Space is the one way in. `tools/checks/arm64-voicemode-check.py` covers it on QEMU virt, with a tone in place of a microphone and stubs for the relay and the words service. |
| `arch/arm64/ask.c` (voice) | Samantha's voice on the Pi: a `[[say TEXT]]` action prints the caption, posts the text to the relay's `/api/tts` (TLS on the Pi, the same token) and boxes the 22050 Hz 16-bit clip to the app sound path's 8-bit mono, queued from `ask_poll` as the ring makes room, capped at 8 seconds. A relay refusal prints `voice: no audio (N)`. `tools/checks/arm64-voice-check.py` runs it on virt against a stub relay. |
| `arch/arm64/ask.c` (voice mode) | Double-tap Space and hold: `vm_key` watches the taps in the key handler, `vm_poll` confirms the hold 250 ms later from the main loop, swallows the two spaces, arms the microphone ring (`usb_mic_arm`, `usb_mic_take`, `usb_mic_peak` in `xhci.c`), draws a `listening [####......]` cue with a live level and asks `main.c` for the menu bar's terracotta mic mark (`menubar_mic`); the release hands the samples to `hear_post`. `tools/checks/arm64-voicemode-check.py` holds the key through QMP on virt. |
| `arch/arm64/main.c` (the chat view's face) | `con_tui_begin` gives the chat view seven rows of face when the window has fourteen or more: Samantha's portrait from `face_art.h` (`tools/gen/gen_face_art.py`, two frames of `landing/face`, mouth closed and open), the two mixed by `voice_level` (`face_blit`) (ask.c: the loudness of her clip at the point the sound path has reached), repainted 25 times a second through `con_tui_mouth` while she speaks. `tools/checks/arm64-voice-check.py` wants `face: on` and a moving `face: mouth=` on virt. |
| `arch/arm64/fptest.c` | Test support for the ARM64 floating point check, linked into the `fpsave`, `fpnosave` and (since M3e) `musictest` builds only, never the real kernel. In `musictest` its `fp_clobber` wipes the FPU inside every app call and before every resume, so `fpkeep` proves an app's FP registers are kept (`tools/checks/arm64-music-check.py`). It loads known values into q0-q31, FPCR and FPSR, waits for timer interrupts, and stands in for an interrupt handler that uses floating point by wiping them all. `tools/checks/arm64-fp-check.py` proves `vectors.S` brings them back, and that the build without the save fails. |
| `tools/wifi-fw.sh` | Fetches the three CYW43455 firmware files (`brcmfmac43455-sdio.bin`, `.txt`, `.clm_blob`) plus the licence `copyright` into `build/wifi-fw/` (gitignored), pinned to one RPi-Distro/firmware-nonfree commit, size-checked, all four moved in together so a dropped download leaves nothing half-written. Run by `make -C arch/arm64 pi` and `tools/flash-pi.sh`. The files are Cypress's and are never committed. |
| `tools/bt-fw.sh` | Fetches the Bluetooth patch `BCM4345C0.hcd` and `LICENSE.cypress` into `build/bt-fw/` (gitignored), pinned to one RPi-Distro/bluez-firmware commit, size-checked, both moved in together. Run by `make -C arch/arm64 pi` (`bt-fw`) and `tools/flash-pi.sh`. The patch is Cypress's and is never committed. |
| `arch/arm64/wifi_proto.h` | The pure half of the Wi-Fi driver: SDPCM and BCDC frame packing, escan result parsing and NVRAM packing, with no hardware access so `tools/checks/wifi-host-check.sh` can test it on the host. Included by `wifi.c`. |
| `drivers/lib_caveatbrush_font.h`, `drivers/lib_comicneue_font.h`, `drivers/lib_dancingscript_font.h`, `drivers/lib_ebgaramond_font.h`, `drivers/lib_jetbrainsmono_font.h`, `drivers/lib_sourcesans3_font.h`, `drivers/lib_sourceserif4_font.h` | The font library: seven open-licence typefaces (hand lettering, a comic face, a script, a book serif, a coding face, two reading faces) as data the font registry can load, so the OS is not stuck with one look. Each is a generated table, not hand-written code. |
| `arch/arm64/wpa.h` | The WPA2 half of Wi-Fi: SHA-1, HMAC, the pairwise-key PRF and AES key unwrap, so Joshua Tree does the 4-way handshake itself (the Pi's chip has no supplicant). `tools/checks/wpa-test.c` checks it against the RFC vectors. |
| `arch/arm64/wifi_cfg.sh` | Writes the build file `arch/arm64/wifi_cfg.h` (gitignored, never in git): the network name and its key (worked out at build time, never the passphrase) from `~/.config/joshuatree/wifi.conf`, only when `JT_WIFI_DEV=1` (cards from `tools/flash-pi.sh`). Release and CI builds carry no network. |
| `tools/checks/wpa-test.c` | Host test for `wpa.h` against the SHA-1, HMAC-SHA1, PRF and RFC 3394 key-unwrap test vectors; run by the suite. |
| `tools/gen/sample-song.py` | Writes `HELLO.WAV`, the sample song: 8 bars at 120 bpm in C major (bass, arpeggio pad and lead), 16 s, 16-bit mono 22050 Hz, about 690 KB, pure arithmetic so every run is byte-identical. `tools/flash-pi.sh` puts it in `JT/MUSIC/` only when the folder has no `.WAV` or `.MP3`; `tools/checks/arm64-music-check.py` plays it as a third song. |
| `tools/gen/map.py` | Draws `architecture.svg`, the one-picture map (where it runs, the stack, the Samantha flow, the Pi checklist, the build loop, memory, apps over time). Reads apps and memory from the build, so they cannot drift. |
| `tools/gen/breadboard.py` | Blender script that renders the breadboard prototype picture in `docs/hardware/`; the wiring it shows is written in `docs/GUIDE.md` (Breadboard prototype). |
| `docs/hardware/nimbus_cad.py` | Nimbus, a small square computer for a Pi 4 and the 7 inch touch display. build123d lofts the front outline from the icon squircle (n = 4.8) into a front shell and a back cover on a 0.5 mm reveal, cuts the finger scoop on top, the glass ledge, the debossed tree, the SD slot and the rear port strip, adds the Pi standoffs, ribs and screws, and writes per-part STLs and STEPs into `docs/hardware/nimbus/`. Every fit (bed size, display and window clearances, standoffs) is asserted before a file is written. |
| `docs/hardware/render_nimbus.py` | Nimbus product renders from the real CAD: builds the parts, then headless Cycles with one overhead soft key on a cream sweep, the same light and camera height on every view, the desktop picture on the screen, 2x supersampled to `nimbus-hero.jpg`, `nimbus-rear.jpg` and `nimbus-side.jpg`. |
| `docs/hardware/nimbus_desktop.py` | The picture on Nimbus's screen: a saved 1920 x 1080 desktop screendump of the Pi build with Weather, Calendar and Notes composed on it from `landing/shots/` (`--compose`), or a fresh screendump from QEMU's raspi4b model, written to `docs/hardware/nimbus/nimbus-desktop.png`. |
| `docs/hardware/nimbus_blueprint.py` | The Nimbus print blueprint: projects the tessellated parts into front, side, rear, top, underside and exploded views with the overall sizes, M3 screws, vent, bed fit and print settings, and writes `nimbus/nimbus-blueprint.svg` and `.pdf`. |
| `docs/hardware/nimbus_box.py` | The Nimbus retail box render: a plain white two-piece box with the tree mark and the name on the lid, same light and sweep as the product views, to `nimbus-box.jpg`. |
| `docs/hardware/nimbus_pack.py` | The Nimbus print pack in `nimbus/pack/`: STLs checked against a 256 mm bed (`stl-check.json`), a 1:1 blueprint on A3 and tiled on A4 and Letter, a bill of materials, a per-part print sheet, six assembly step drawings and a print-shop sheet. |
| `docs/hardware/pi4b.py` | The Raspberry Pi 4B's numbers from its mechanical drawing: board, mounting holes, standoff, tallest port and the port windows (their heights measured from the top of the board). Constants only. `nimbus_cad.py` and `samantha_cad.py` both import it, so one caliper fix reaches both cases. |
| `docs/hardware/samantha_cad.py` | The Samantha box, a desk unit for a 7 inch screen, a Pi 4B and a speaker. build123d writes the back shell, the bezel, a two-cradle stand and a fit-test tray, plus a STEP of the shell and bezel. Its fit asserts (bosses, ports, screen, grille, the box in its stand) all run before a file is written. See `docs/GUIDE.md` (Samantha box). |
| `docs/hardware/render_samantha.py`, `docs/hardware/render_kit.py` | The Samantha box previews: build the CAD into a temp folder, render the shell and bezel in Blender's Workbench with no window, and shrink them to the two JPGs with magick. `render_kit.py` is the build-then-Blender start and the colour and material helpers, shared with `render_nimbus.py`. |
| `tools/checks/samantha-box-check.py` | Runs `samantha_cad.py` into a scratch folder and fails if a fit assert stops it or one of its five files is missing. A copy with the old 130.7 mm stand slot must stop and write nothing. Skips without uv and a cached build123d. |
| `tools/checks/arm64-wifi-check.py` | Boots the Pi image on QEMU's raspi4b model, with and without the firmware files, and asserts `M1c fb ok`, `wifi power on`, `wifi FAIL cmd5` and then `usb pcie absent`, so a Wi-Fi step that waits forever fails CI. Skips where clang, lld, qemu or the raspi4b model is missing. |
| `tools/checks/arm64-bt-check.py` | Boots the virt build with a second serial port on a Unix socket to a fake H4 controller and a made-up `.hcd` (`BT_HCD=`). Asserts the command order, every patch record whole and in file order, the 921600 baud command, LE Meta in the event mask, the version line, and that `bt scan` lists two devices in the order heard with names merged from a scan response and a later advert, past a stray ACL packet. Then `bt pair` against a fake mouse with ramfb on: the check's own AES-128 (tested against FIPS-197 and the spec's c1 and s1 samples) proves the kernel's confirm is c1 of its random and its Start Encryption key is s1; one ACL credit proves flow control; a GATT database with paged, fragmented replies and a CCCD that is not value + 1; the mouse's own requests answered; then four boot reports move the pointer onto the Mail tile and click it. A wrong confirm value ends in `bt: pairing failed` and a hang-up. No key, random or confirm value ever reaches the UART. Then a controller that wants another record order and one that never answers Read Local Version: one `bt: no answer from the radio` line and the Terminal still answers. Then no second serial port: `bt: no radio`, no crash. Then the pairing is saved to a FAT32 virtio card and the next boots run on copies of it: a reconnect with the saved LTK, Rand and EDIV (no scan, no SMP), a mouse that never answers (a 12 s try, record kept, `bt status` says searching with the address masked, then `bt forget`), a mouse that ignores boot protocol and sends packed 12 bit X and Y (its report map is read and parsed), a mouse asleep at boot that wakes on the second try and one that drops the link later (each reconnects with no typing, the notifications are written again and the pointer moves), a mouse that rejects the key (record erased) and a card with no record. |
| `tools/checks/bt-pair-check.py` | Builds `btpair.h` on the host with clang and sanitizers and round-trips a record with an obviously fake key; refuses bad magic, another version, every truncation, any flipped byte, a zero key and a bad address type; checks that no printed string holds the key in either byte order and that `bt.c` is wired to the file, `bt forget` and the saved pairing line. The reconnect itself needs the Pi; `arm64-bt-check.py` runs it against a fake mouse. |
| `kernel/exec.c` | Loads a flat binary off the filesystem into a fixed window the linker script reserves, marks its pages user-accessible, and starts it as a ring-3 task with argv. |
| `user/hello.c` + `user/jtsys.h` | The reference user program and the one header a user program gets: inline `int 0x80` wrappers, nothing else. Since 2.49.0 the header also has an `svc #0` path for aarch64 (x8 number, x0 to x2 arguments, same `JT_SYS_*` numbers and -errno results), and `hello.c` and `calendar.c` build unchanged for the ARM kernel (`arch/arm64/userapps.S`). |
| `user/note.c` | The second user program, and the first one worth running: prints a file, appends a line, seeks. Exercises the v2 syscalls. |
| `user/libjt/` | A small C library for user programs: `string.c`, `stdlib.c` (`malloc` over `SYS_BRK`, a fixed arena when brk is refused; its pointer casts have an aarch64 form since 2.59.0, the i386 bytes the same), `stdio.c` (`printf` and friends over the write syscall), `text.c` (antialiased DejaVu text drawn into the app's window), plus `ctype.h` and `unistd.h`. Built into `libjt.a`. |
| `user/libjt/text.c`, `user/libjt/text.h` | Antialiased text for ring-3 apps (1.9.23). `jt_text_draw` applies the shared ink curve, then blends each glyph's coverage over the pixels already in the window, in a body face, a real bold and a large digit face. `text.h` is the API: draw, width, height, ascent. |
| `user/libjt/mono.c`, `user/libjt/aamono.h` | The mono face for ring-3 apps: DejaVu Sans Mono, ASCII only, one size, every glyph an 8 px advance. `jt_mono_draw` uses its own generated atlas (`aamono.h`, baked by `tools/gen/gen_user_text.c` next to `aafont.h`) and lives in its own object so only the Terminal pays for it. |
| `user/libjt/jpeg.c` | One line: `#define JPEG_USER` then `#include "../../drivers/jpeg.c"`, so ring 3 runs the kernel face decoder itself. `jpeg_decode_scaled` decodes one MCU row at a time into a box-averaged RGB565 frame, which is how Samantha decodes a 320x320 JPEG frame to the 60x60 the kernel face shows; the JPEGs themselves live in her `SYS_BRK` heap. `jpeg_decode_scaled32` (2.9.0) is the same walk into packed 0RGB, up to 1024 wide, for her 736 pixel portrait. |
| `user/libjt/png.c` | Two lines: `#define PNG_USER` then the kernel's `drivers/inflate.c` and `drivers/png.c`, so ring 3 decodes PNG with the one decoder the kernel has (buffers from libjt's malloc, its statics in `.data`). Music reads a PNG cover with it, cropped to the centre square and box-averaged to 96 px; a picture over 1 megapixel is refused before any buffer is asked for. |
| `tools/gen/typetokens.py`, the Type system section | The type system, one source: nine sizes (11 to 64), line heights on a 4 px grid, tracking by size, weights with jobs, one spacing ladder. It writes the token block and `@font-face` rules into `landing/index.html` and the generated the Type system section; `--snap` moved every landing font size onto the scale. |
| `tools/fonts/treesans/build.py` | Builds Tree Sans from Inter 4.1 (OFL): Text and Display optical sizes, frozen alternates, vertical metrics on a 1/16 em grid, renamed, TTF and WOFF2. The faces are `landing/fonts/TreeSans*.woff2` and `tools/fonts/treesans/TreeSans*.ttf`. |
| `tools/checks/type-grid-check.py` | Fails when a landing font size is off the scale, the token block or the Type system section is stale, or a Tree Sans file does not load. |
| `user/libjt/wav.c`, `wav.h` | The WAV reader for the Music app. `wav_open` checks a RIFF/WAVE file in memory (8 or 16-bit PCM, mono or stereo, 4000 to 44100 Hz) and refuses anything else; a data size that lies is clamped to the file. `wav_read8` returns mono 8-bit unsigned samples, the format `SYS_AUDIO` takes. It never reads outside the buffer it is given. |
| `user/libjt/avi.c`, `avi.h` | The AVI reader for the Movie app: motion-JPEG video and optional 8/16-bit PCM sound, parsed from a file held in memory. `avi_next` hands back video and audio chunks in file order, steps into `rec ` groups, ignores streams it does not play, and treats a cut-off last chunk as the end. `avi_audio8` makes audio chunks into the 8-bit mono `SYS_AUDIO` takes. |
| `tools/media/avigen.py`, `avi-host.c` | The fixtures and host test for `avi.c`: real JPEG frames through the kernel decoder, truncation, bad headers and a mutation fuzz under ASan and UBSan. Run by `tools/checks/avi-host-check.sh`. |
| `tools/media/demo-music.py`, `demo-disk.sh` | Three original demo songs for the Music app, synthesized from scratch (so nothing to license): 8-bit mono 11025 Hz WAV, about 40 s and 430KB each. `demo-disk.sh` puts them in `MUSIC/` on a FAT disk image. They are generated, never committed, and never embedded in the kernel image. |
| `tools/checks/fpu-check.sh` | Boots the kernel and runs `fputest`: two tasks keep different values on the x87 stack across task switches. Proves `schedule()` swaps per-task float state, which the float-heavy MP3 decoder needs. |
| `tools/media/wavgen.py`, `wav-host.c` | The fixtures and the host test for `wav.c`: golden samples, broken headers, then 20000 mutations under ASan and UBSan. Run by `tools/checks/media-host-check.sh`. |
