| `tools/media/musicfix.py` | Builds the FAT16 disk `tools/checks/music-check.py` boots with: a 440Hz WAV in the root, a 16-bit stereo 660Hz WAV and a 3.3 MB over-the-cap WAV in `MUSIC/`. The songs live on the disk, never in the kernel image. `--set qa`, `many` and `empty` build the libraries `music-qa-check.py` boots: songs that will not play, an MP3 with one damaged frame, a 3:25 MP3 made of the fixture repeated, fourteen 1 s songs, or none. |
| `tools/checks/music-qa-check.py` | The Music QA pass (2.44.1), three headless boots with a Sound Blaster and the `musicfix.py` disks. It clicks songs, presses keys, sends a real wheel step and snaps the window, and reads the app's `music:` serial lines and the framebuffer: a 3:25 MP3 reads 3:25 and seeks near its end, a damaged MP3 frame no longer hangs the song, an unplayable song is skipped when one ends into the next and n moves on from it, the queue stays under half a second ahead, shuffle plays each song once, the wheel scrolls the list, no row sits under the key hint, a half-screen window keeps its pills, and an empty library is quiet. Each fix's assertion was run against the code it replaced and failed; the position-clock check after a seek is a drift proof that never failed. |
| `third_party/minimp3/minimp3.h` | Vendored minimp3 (CC0 public domain, https://github.com/lieff/minimp3): a single-header MP3 decoder for Layer I, II and III files. Includes stubs `stdlib.h` and `string.h` that redirect to `user/libjt/`. |
| `user/libjt/mp3.c`, `mp3.h` | The MP3 reader for the Music app (2.2). `mp3_open` skips an ID3v2 tag, finds the first frame and adds up every frame's samples with a header-only scan for the length. `mp3_read8` hands back mono 8-bit unsigned samples and keeps the tail of a frame that straddles two calls. `mp3_seek` scans to the frame, then decodes about 2KB of earlier frames and drops them, because a frame leans on the bit reservoir of the ones before it. A frame the decoder cannot decode (damaged side info, or a song cut out of a stream whose first frames lean on data it never had) plays as the silence its header says it lasts, so the read always matches the length the scan counted; it used to stop there and the player hung. minimp3 keeps about 15KB of float tables on its stack and a ring-3 program has 4KB, so the decoder state, frame buffers and a 64KB stack of its own live in one heap block and the decode runs on that stack. |
| `tools/media/mp3-host.c`, `fixtures/` | The host test for `mp3.c`: golden length, the 440 Hz pitch and amplitude band, chunked reads equal one read, seek equals playing through, truncated and garbage input, a mutation fuzz, all under ASan and UBSan. Fixtures are three small committed MP3s (`fixtures/regen.sh` rebuilds them with ffmpeg), so CI needs no ffmpeg. |
| `user/libjt/avi.c`, `avi.h` | The AVI reader for Movies. `avi_open` checks a RIFF/AVI file in memory (motion-JPEG video, optional 8 or 16-bit PCM sound) and refuses anything else; `avi_next` hands back the video and audio chunks in file order, `avi_audio8` turns audio bytes into 8-bit mono. A chunk that runs past the end of the file is the end of the movie. It never reads outside the buffer it is given. |
| `tools/media/avigen.py`, `avi-host.c`, `tools/checks/avi-host-check.sh` | The fixtures and the host test for `avi.c`: real JPEG frames through the real decoder, odd chunk orders, broken headers, then mutations under ASan and UBSan. `avigen.py --movie` also builds the clips `movie-check.py` plays (a 2 s clip whose frame number is drawn as squares, a damaged clip, a non-AVI, one over the size cap). |
| `user/libjt/osk.c`, `user/libjt/osk.h` | The phone on-screen keyboard for ring-3 apps. Five rows of 40 px at the window bottom (digits, three letter rows with backspace, then space, enter and done), drawn with the antialiased text face. `jt_osk_draw` paints it, `jt_osk_hit` turns a click into a key code (done answers Esc), `jt_osk_height` is what an app subtracts from its text area. The launcher passes `phone` as argv[1] when the kernel booted in phone mode; Notes is the first user. |
| `user/libjt/aafont.h` | Generated glyph atlas behind `text.c`: 4-bit coverage bitmaps of DejaVu Sans, rendered from the kernel's own font data. Rebuilt by the Makefile when the font or the generator changes. |
| `tools/gen/gen_user_text.c` | Host program that bakes `user/libjt/aafont.h` through `drivers/ttf.c`, so ring-3 text and in-kernel text share one rasterizer and one font. |
| `arch/arm64/screen_idle.h` | Pi display idle timer: blank HDMI after five minutes, keep active Brick and assistant requests awake, resume on keyboard or mouse without stopping apps or USB polling. Mailbox failures retain the previous state. |
| `arch/arm64/notice.h` | One bounded menu message; a newer one replaces it and it expires after four seconds, including timer wrap. |
| `arch/arm64/brick.h`, `arch/arm64/brick_ui.h`, `tools/checks/arm64-brick-check.py` | Original brick-breaker rules and ARM window: 32 bricks, three lives, arrows or mouse, pause and restart. Hardware-counter animation; host physics and QEMU input/pixel checks. |
| `arch/arm64/ufile.c` (`ufile_sync`), `arch/arm64/power.h` (`power_halt_screen`), `arch/arm64/main.c` (`POWER_FILE_TEST`) | 4.16.0 power safety. `power_apply` calls `ufile_sync` first, which writes every open descriptor with unwritten bytes to the card (Notes mid-save), then a shutdown paints the safe-to-unplug screen. On the board (54 MHz counter) it then stays on that screen; under QEMU the watchdog halts the model. `reboot`, `shutdown`, the chords and the tree menu rows all end here, and none asks for sudo. The `powerfile` and `powernoflush` builds drive it: a file left unsaved, `reboot`, read back, `shutdown`; the second build skips the flush and must lose the file. |
| `arch/arm64/power.h`, `tools/checks/arm64-power-check.py` | Pi keyboard shutdown and restart, explicit confirmation and cancellation, VideoCore SD power-off and watchdog reset; host safety checks and real raspi4b shutdown/reboot tests. |
| `user/finder.c`, `tools/checks/arm64-finder-check.py` | Finder on the ARM64 build (3.14.0), the file window of the Burrow tile and Spotlight (`SPOT_FINDER`), a ring-3 program built only for aarch64. A 132 px sidebar (Home, then Documents, Music and Trash when `JT/` holds them; an 8.3 card's `DOCUME~1` counts as Documents), a list of 24 px rows with a flat 16 px glyph per type (folder in the accent `#b5502c`, document, music, image, no text in an icon), the name and the size. Up and Down choose, Enter opens a folder or previews a `.TXT` read-only through `SYS_READFILE` (2 KB, wrapped, since Notes takes no path), Backspace goes up, Left and Right swap the sidebar and the list, Esc closes. It reads through `SYS_READDIR` relative to `JT/`; with no card it lists the built-in apps and the status line says so. Its arena sits past `_user_bss_end`, not `_user_end`, because the linker script puts the `.bss` right after the image. Check: boots with a card holding folders and a file, opens Finder from Spotlight, lists, goes into a folder and back, previews a note, moves in the sidebar, closes; then boots with no card and opens it through `burrow`. |
| `tools/checks/arm64-spotlight-check.py` | Boots the ARM kernel headless in QEMU, presses F1: an empty query lists all 18 apps, typing calc shows exactly Calculator, Enter opens its window. |
| `tools/gen/ci-balance.py` | Rebalances the CI shards. Reads how long each check took in a finished run and rewrites the shard numbers in `tools/checks/ci-suite.sh`: a check that runs qemu-system-aarch64 or builds arch/arm64 goes on an ARM shard, the rest on the others, longest first. The ARM shards and the count come from `.github/workflows/check.yml`. |
| `tools/checks/ci-tooling-check.py` | Host fixtures prove the balancer calls a check ARM by what it runs (the ARM QEMU or an arch/arm64 build), packs those across the ARM shards and the rest off them, that check.yml, ci-local.sh and SHARDS agree on one ARM list and shard count, and that a fixed test path stops local CI before builds. |
| `tools/ci-local.sh`, `tools/hooks/pre-push` | Run the fixed-path guard before builds; local CI then runs the release suite with two workers. |
| `tools/ci-lock.sh` | One suite at a time on this Mac. `ci-local.sh` and `ci-suite.sh` source it: an atomic `mkdir` lock with a pid file, stale locks cleared, a 60 minute wait, released on exit. `CI_LOCAL_NO_LOCK=1` skips it. |
| `tools/checks/scratch.py` | Gives each QEMU check its own temp directory, removed at exit. `JT_KEEP_TMP=1` keeps it for debugging. |
| `tools/checks/tmp-paths-check.py`, `tools/checks/tmp-paths-baseline.txt` | Fails when a check hard-codes a fixed `/tmp/jt-*` path or socket. The baseline lists the older offenders and only ever shrinks. |
| `tools/checks/relay-tts-check.py` | Proves the relay's voice endpoint (2.45.0) against a stub ElevenLabs server on loopback, with no network and no money: the stub's PCM comes back, text over the per-request cap and requests past the per-day cap are refused with no upstream call, the day count survives a relay restart, a new date starts fresh, a failed call gives its characters back, the key never shows in any response, header or log (even when the stub echoes it), a tokenless request is 401 and a key file others can read is refused. |
| `tools/claude-relay/relay.py`, `tools/checks/claude-relay-check.py` | The relay behind the Claude app (2.14.0), also Samantha's voice (2.45.0): `POST /api/tts` with the same token and TLS rules returns 22050 Hz mono 16-bit PCM from ElevenLabs, with caps and a private key file (`docs/GUIDE.md` (The Claude app)), Python standard library only. It takes `POST /api/claude` with a shared token and runs headless Claude Code (`claude -p`) with read-only tools in the repo, one request at a time, with a hard timeout. It is a command-capable agent behind an HTTP port: it binds loopback unless told `--lan`. The check proves the token, session, argv, timeout, size and logging rules against a stub `claude`. LAN binding requires a TLS certificate and key; loopback HTTP is retained for i386/QEMU. `docs/GUIDE.md` (Encrypted Pi relay) covers the card and certificate setup. API file tools open relative to a pinned directory with no symlink following, check the opened file is regular and refuse replacements without blocking; `relay-api-check.py` covers symlink, FIFO and deletion races. `docs/GUIDE.md` (The Claude app) explains it. |
| the Design rules section, `tools/checks/design-doc-check.py` | The design system as one page of written rules (2.12.2): what the OS never does, the shared icon shape and light, the fleet-icon rule, type, colour and day and night, window and dock numbers, and the caption timings. The check reads every `NAME = value` in the page and compares it with the file that defines it, so a rule cannot drift from the code; it also scans `art/icons` for text, purple, teal and gradient hue. |
| `tools/landing-shots.py`, `tools/landing_shots_manifest.py`, `tools/checks/landing-shots-fresh-check.py` | The landing app tiles in `landing/shots/`. The script retakes a tile headlessly (the fleet apps boot with `open=<app>` and no network, so they show their offline samples) and records a hash of the sources that draw it in `landing/shots/sources.json`. The check fails when an app changed and its tile was not retaken. |
| `landing/hero/samantha.webp`, `landing/hero/samantha-phone.webp`, `tools/checks/hero-poster-check.mjs` | The hero poster (2.11.1): real stills of Samantha's full-screen view, 1920x1080 for desktop and tablet and 860x1520 (430x760 at 2x) for phones, captured from the booted kernel's own canvas so lifting the poster on the first click shows the same picture, alive. `#hero-poster` in `landing/index.html` covers the stage; `landing/v86/embed.js` lifts it and takes the keyboard (the machine boots behind it from page load, with the `samantha` token on its command line unless `?desktop`). The check proves the poster is a real undistorted portrait by pixels, the default boot reaches her face, Esc closes her to the desktop, `?desktop` opts out, phone and tablet do not scroll sideways, and that it fails when the poster is removed. |
| `user/wc.c` | Unix `wc`, counts lines, words and bytes. The first program linked against libjt instead of raw syscalls; installed as WC.BIN at boot for the text shell. |
| `user/brkpoke.c` | The `SYS_BRK` leak probe (1.9.27): grows 3 MB, touches every page, checks each came back zero, shrinks, then crashes on purpose so `tools/checks/ring3brk-check.py` can assert the PMM free count and brk live count return to baseline. Run by `kernel/ring3app.c` under the `brkpoke` boot flag. |
| `user/fbpoke.c` | The program that must not work (1.7.8): runs after a window closed, hands `write` a kernel pointer and a pointer into the released framebuffer (both must be `-EFAULT`), then stores into it and must page-fault. Run by `kernel/ring3app.c` under the `fbpoke` boot flag. |
| `user/keyrate.c` | Keyrate, the typing test, as a ring-3 program: the first app to leave the kernel (1.7.7). Gets its window from `SYS_WINDOW_OPEN`, its keys from `SYS_WINDOW_POLL`, draws its own 8x16 glyphs. The backquote key crashes it on purpose. |
| `user/toroid.c` | Toroid, Conway's Life on a torus, as a ring-3 program: the second app out of the kernel (1.7.11). Two 160x80 bit-packed boards in its own .data, generations paced off `SYS_TIME`, the backquote key crashes it on purpose. |
| `user/calculator.c` | Calculator, a recursive-descent parser over `+ - * / ()`, as a ring-3 program: the third app out of the kernel (1.7.12). Same grammar as the in-kernel version, evaluated straight into a `double` per rule instead of an `expr_node` tree, since a flat binary has no `.bss` and no `kmalloc`. Dividing by zero yields 0, unchanged. The backquote key crashes it on purpose. Tab switches to scientific: `^`, `!`, `%`, pi, e, ans and sin through exp, on the x87 with no libm. |
| `user/quotes.c` | Quotes, the film-quote guessing game, as a ring-3 program: the fourth app out of the kernel (1.7.14). Same fixed deck and answer-rotation as the in-kernel version, streak and best kept in its own `.data`. The backquote key crashes it on purpose. |
| `user/bookrank.c` | Bookrank, the ranked non-fiction shelf, as a ring-3 program: the fifth app out of the kernel (2.0). Two panes, up/down or a click selects, the list scrolls. The top twelve books come live from the real Bookrank API through `SYS_HTTP_GET` and the Worker (2.6.26); the reply is untrusted, so every byte is forced printable and every copy is bounded. Ten built-in samples show when the fetch or the parse fails. The backquote key crashes it on purpose. |
| `user/tonchi.c` | Patois (was Patois, was Lexly), learn anything, as a ring-3 program: the seventh app out of the kernel (1.9.1). A course picker over the live Patois courses, then a four-choice drill with a score (2.6.27), through `SYS_HTTP_GET` and the Worker. The reply is untrusted: bytes forced printable, every copy bounded, answer index checked, bad rows dropped. With no network or a bad reply it opens the 30-word Spanish deck as before. Keys 1 to 4 or a click answer, Esc goes back to the picker. The backquote key crashes it on purpose for `tools/checks/ring3tonchi-check.py`; `tools/checks/ring3tonchi-live-check.py` proves the live and offline paths. |
| `user/fieldbook.c` | Fieldbook, every field of science and math explained plainly, as a ring-3 program: the ninth app out of the kernel (1.9.3). Same twelve fields and two-pane layout as the old in-kernel copy, up/down or a click selects, the explanation word-wraps by character count in the 8x16 font, and backquote is the deliberate crash `tools/checks/ring3fieldbook-check.py` presses. |
| `user/clock.c` | Clock, the time of day, a countdown timer and one alarm, as a ring-3 program: the tenth app out of the kernel (1.9.4). Same three jobs as the old in-kernel copy, the time comes from `SYS_TIME`, and the timer minutes and alarm are typed on the program's own line since a ring-3 program has no prompt box. Backquote is the deliberate crash `tools/checks/ring3clock-check.py` can press. |
| `user/music.c` | Music, a song player as a ring-3 program with its own window (2.2): the library lists every `.WAV` in the Files root and in a `MUSIC` folder (`SYS_READDIR`), one song at a time is read whole into the `SYS_BRK` heap (`SYS_READFILE`, refused over 3 MB with a message) and `wav.c` turns it into the 8-bit mono the card takes. Play, pause, previous, next, a seek bar (click or arrow keys), shuffle, repeat, a software volume and the wheel on the list. `.MP3` goes through `user/libjt/mp3.c`. The elapsed time is the stream start plus the driver's `played` counter, never the wall clock (32-bit math split into seconds and a remainder, so a song over 97 s at 44.1 kHz does not wrap), and pause or seek wait for the queue to drain first so that number stays true. About a third of a second is kept queued, not more, because the volume is applied before a sample is queued. Shuffle plays every song once a round (repeat off stops, repeat all starts another); when one song ends into the next a song that will not play is skipped, while n and p stop on it and say why. A narrow window puts the Shuffle and Repeat pills on their own row. Backquote is the deliberate crash for `tools/checks/ring3crash-all-check.py`; `tools/checks/music-check.py` and `music-qa-check.py` read its `music:` serial lines. Since 2.59.0 the same file runs on the ARM kernel (M3e): Spotlight opens it, its library is the card's `JT/MUSIC/`, and `tools/checks/arm64-music-check.py` plays a WAV and an MP3 with it on QEMU virt. |
| `user/movies.c` | Movies, a motion-JPEG AVI player with sound, as a ring-3 program (2.2). Lists `.AVI` files from Files, reads the whole clip into its `SYS_BRK` heap with `SYS_READ_FILE`, indexes it once with `avi.c`, then plays with the audio as the clock: the frame on screen is `played / rate / frame time`, only that frame is decoded, late frames are dropped and the picture never makes the sound wait. Space pauses, left and right seek five seconds, a click on the bar seeks, `f` hides the controls and letterboxes into the whole window. A clip too big, damaged or not an AVI shows a message instead. Backquote is the deliberate crash `ring3crash-all-check.py` presses; `tools/checks/movie-check.py` proves the rest. |
| `user/hamurabi.c` | Hamurapi (Hamurabi in the sources), the 1968 city game, as a ring-3 program with its own window (2.7), playable start to finish. The title screen (three buttons: story, Classic 1968, a demo the robot king plays by itself), a card at the gate of some story years (the opening, a turning point in years 5 and 10, or an omen with two choices, one drawn grey when the city cannot pay), the orders panel (Land, Feed, Plant, each with minus, plus and a bar you can click; Up and Down pick a row, Left and Right change it, Shift takes big steps, Enter ends the year; the orders are clamped like the web game's so a year can never be illegal), the year's report and the ending with the grade. The drawn city behind it (sky, hills, town around a ziggurat with the king on top, river and boat, fields, sheep, walking villagers) reacts as the web scene does: a villager for every four people, plots sown for what you order planted, sacks for the grain, the harvest and rats as floating numbers, ghosts for the starved, a green fog for the plague, rain on a ruined reign, fireworks on a great one. It draws at the largest whole-number pixel scale the window allows. The sprite sheet is unpacked once onto the `SYS_BRK` heap and every frame is painted into a back buffer, then copied over the window, so the compositor never catches half a frame. Each reign seeds its dice from the clock and logs its seed and every year's orders and result on the serial port; the backquote key crashes it on purpose. `tools/checks/ring3hamurabi-check.py` proves it, replaying every logged year on the host. |
| `user/hamurabi_sprites.h` | Hamurabi's 125 sprites on one 256x149 sheet: a 133-colour palette, the sheet as run-length coded palette indices (about 21 KB instead of 38 KB) and an enum of sprite names with their rectangles. Generated by `tools/gen/gen_hamurabi_sprites.py` from `art/hamurabi/sprites.png` and `sprites.json`, committed, and checked for staleness in the suite. |
| `user/windgate.c` | Airwell, guided breathing, as a ring-3 program (2.8). The web app's four presets (Box 4-4-4-4, 4-7-8, Relax, Coherent): a circle that grows on the in-breath, holds, and shrinks on the out-breath, the phase word, the seconds left, a round counter and a session timer. Each frame is painted into a back buffer on the `SYS_BRK` heap and copied over the window in one pass. Time comes from `SYS_TIME` (true wall seconds); the tick counter only places it inside the second and is re-learned at every boundary, because QEMU's runs about twice too fast. Exercise time stops while paused. It writes one serial line per phase start with the exact boundary time, which `tools/checks/ring3windgate-check.py` verifies. Backquote is the deliberate crash `ring3crash-all-check.py` presses. |
| `user/panes.c` | Panes, a cmux-style terminal multiplexer, as a ring-3 program (2.11.0), beside the Terminal rather than inside it. A sans tab rail on the left (up to nine tabs, named for the last command or folder, an accent dot on a tab that printed while you were elsewhere), each tab holding one or two panes side by side or stacked. Every pane is its own shell: its own 3 KB scrollback, input line, five-entry history and working directory, carved from one 47 KB arena past the image (12 panes in the pool). `cd`, `clear` and the shell commands come from `user/shellcore.h`; `sleep N [text]` is local, the one background timer, which prints when it ends and so lights the dot. Keys: Ctrl+T new tab, Ctrl+W close pane then tab (the last tab resets), Ctrl+1 to 9 and Ctrl+Left/Right switch, Ctrl+D split, Ctrl+E stack, Ctrl+O next pane; clicks on a tab, the New tab row or a pane work too. The kernel hands only this window those chords, as `KEY_CTL_*` (`kernel/app.h`, `key_ctl_code`, in `gui_multiwin_key_nonblock`); the Terminal and every other app keep plain keys. Apps folder, `open=pane`. `tools/checks/panes-check.py` proves it from framebuffer pixels and the `panes: tabs/panes/job done` serial markers. |
| `user/shellcore.h` | The shell engine the Terminal and Panes share (2.11.0): the echoed prompt line, `cd` against `jt_readdir`, `clear`, and the `SYS_SHELL_RUN` round trip with its `terminal: ran=N` serial line. Header-only and callback-driven (each app owns its scrollback and input), so a fix lands in both. |
| `user/claude.c` | Claude, a front end for Claude Code, as a ring-3 program (2.14.0). Claude Code itself runs on the relay machine (`tools/claude-relay/relay.py`), because this OS has no TLS and no Node. You type a question, the app posts it with `SYS_HTTP_POST` and `JT_POST_CLAUDE`, and the answer comes back as plain text: a transcript with You and Claude turns, word-wrapped and scrollable, a thinking line while it waits, and a red line saying why when it fails (no relay set, relay down, wrong token, too slow). It keeps the session id the relay hands back, so a conversation carries on; `/new` starts over. It never sees the relay's address or token: Settings owns both and the kernel adds the token. Apps folder, `open=claude`. `tools/checks/ring3claude-check.py` proves it in QEMU against the real relay and a stub `claude`. |
| `user/books.c` | Books, a plain-text reader as a ring-3 program (2.66), Apps folder and Spotlight, no dock tile. The library lists every `.TXT` in `BOOKS/` (the card's `JT/BOOKS/`), or the Files root when `BOOKS/` is missing. A book is read whole with `SYS_READFILE` into the heap (3 MB cap, refused with a message above it) and paged one wrapped page at a time: Right or Space next, Left back, Esc to the library. Each page turn saves the page to `NAME.POS`, read back on open. `tools/checks/books-check.py` proves the refusal, the page turns and the reopen on a FAT image. `open=books` opens it. |
| `user/mines.c` | Mines, Minesweeper as a ring-3 program: a 9x9 board with 10 mines. A click or space reveals, f flags, arrows move the cursor, r deals a new board, esc closes. The first board of every run comes from a fixed seed, so `tools/checks/ring3mines-check.py` can replay the same game blind and expect a win. Apps folder, `open=mine`. |
| `user/samcaps.h` | Samantha's captions (2.12.1), header-only and `#include`d into `user/samantha.c` to keep that file under the 2000 line ceiling. Up to three lines stacked from just above the glass input, newest at the bottom, each older one a step dimmer; a new line eases up into place over 0.28 s; the whole stack fades together 4 s after the talk goes quiet (0.6 s fade). In a wide window, where her mouth is low, it is one compact row above the input and none at all in a window too short to clear her lips. It prints `samface: line=`, `fade=` and `gone=` with her tick, which `samantha-fullscreen-check.py` reads. |
| `user/activity.c` | Activity, uptime, free memory and the six scheduler slots with a Kill button, as a ring-3 program: the twelfth app out of the kernel (1.9.6). Same screen as the old in-kernel copy, refreshed about once a second through the one new call, `SYS_TASKS`, which also does the kill and refuses the shell and the app itself. Backquote is the deliberate crash `tools/checks/ring3activity-check.py` can press. |
| `user/contacts.c` | Contacts, a list of up to 32 people with a name, phone and email kept in `CONTACTS.TXT`, as a ring-3 program: the thirteenth app out of the kernel (1.9.7). Same list, add prompt, person view and delete as the old in-kernel copy, and the file is rewritten whole after every change through the ordinary open, read, write and close calls, so it needed no new syscall. The list lives in the zeroed pages past the image, since a flat binary has no `.bss`. `tools/checks/ring3contacts-check.py` drives it. |
| `user/hikko.c` | Thinkpond, the idea forum, as a ring-3 program: the fourteenth app out of the kernel (1.9.8, as Sparkjar). Shows the real forum's top twelve ideas (title, votes, text, plan) from `/api/hikko` through `SYS_HTTP_GET` (2.6.29), and the ten demo ideas when the network, the reply or every row fails. Upvotes are local and never sent. `tools/checks/ring3hikko-check.py` drives the demo ideas, `tools/checks/ring3hikko-live-check.py` the live and fallback paths. |
| `user/reminders.c` | Reminders, a checklist of up to 24 items with a done flag kept in `REMINDERS.TXT`, as a ring-3 program: the fifteenth app out of the kernel (1.9.9). Same list, add prompt, tick and delete as the old in-kernel copy, and the file is rewritten whole after every change through the ordinary open, read, write and close calls, so it needed no new syscall. The list lives in the zeroed pages past the image, since a flat binary has no `.bss`. Samantha's reminder tools now read and write the same file fresh on every call from the ring-3 Samantha program. Driven by `tools/checks/ring3reminders-check.py`. |
| `user/curbfind.c` | Curbfind, Craigslist deals near the visitor ranked by deal score with the selected listing's price, neighbourhood, score bar and reason beside them, as a ring-3 program: the sixteenth app out of the kernel (1.9.11). Same list, detail pane and Vancouver samples as the old in-kernel copy. The live rows come through the one new call, `SYS_HTTP_GET`: the kernel fixes the host and port, the program names only the path, and a path that is not plain printable ASCII starting with `/`, or a buffer outside user memory, is refused before the network is touched. The `p` key is the probe `tools/checks/ring3curbfind-check.py` presses to prove those refusals. |
| `user/calendar.c` | Calendar, the Day, Week, Month and Year views with one event per day kept in `EVENTS.TXT`, as a ring-3 program: the seventeenth app out of the kernel (1.9.12). Same views, keys and layout as the old in-kernel copy, drawn in the 8x16 font (the Year view halves it). Today comes from `SYS_TIME`, so the epoch-to-date and weekday math run in the program; `tools/checks/check-calendar.sh` sweeps that math against libc on the host. The file is rewritten whole after every save through the ordinary open, read, write and close calls, so it needed no new syscall. The event list lives in the zeroed pages past the image, since a flat binary has no `.bss`. Driven by `tools/checks/ring3calendar-check.py`. |
| `user/mail.c` | Mail, the inbox list, reader and compose sheet over `MAIL.TXT`, as a ring-3 program: the twenty-second app out of the kernel. Its in-kernel UI is gone; `kernel/mail.h` is now just the message array and load/save. |
| `user/notes.c` | Notes, the folder and note browser plus the in-window editor (wrap, caret, scroll, line copy/cut/paste, Home/End/Delete, Ctrl+S), as a ring-3 program and a compositor window: the twenty-fourth app out of the kernel. Folders and notes are made and removed with `SYS_MKDIR` and `SYS_UNLINK`. A note you have changed saves itself 1 to 2 seconds after your last key (`SYS_TIME`, whole seconds, read only while the note is dirty), because the window's red dot kills the program and Esc never runs; the title's star clears on each save. |
| `user/remindpi.c`, `tools/checks/arm64-reminders-check.py` | Reminders for the ARM build (3.17.0), the Pi's macOS-shaped list: a title and count, rows with round check circles (terracotta `#b5502c` when done, drawn with 4x4 sampling), and a text field along the bottom that is always there. Type and Enter adds; Up walks into the list, Space, Enter or a click on the circle ticks, Backspace or Delete removes the row; done items sink to the bottom. The Makefile builds it as the app named `reminders` (a rule that beats the generic per-app pattern, which would pick the i386 `user/reminders.c`), so the dock tile, Spotlight and `[[open Reminders]]` start it through `app_dock`. It keeps `JT/REMINDER.TXT` (8.3 on FAT) in i386's line format, `0` or `1`, a space, the text, rewritten whole after every change; with no card the open fails and the window says memory only. Check: boots `remw` and `remr` (`NOTES_SELFTEST` 3 and 4) on raspi4b, two adds and a tick on a card (mtools reads the file, fsck is clean), the item back after a second boot, and a no-card boot. |
| `tools/checks/notes-autosave-check.py` | Notes autosave on a headless QEMU guest. Types a line, wants no `notes: saved=` while keys are still coming and none in the first 0.95 s after the last, then exactly one save of the right length, closes through the red dot (reaper line on serial, no `notes: closed`), reads the note off the FAT image with mtools, reopens Notes from the dock and wants the text back on screen. Then builds a private copy with autosave off and wants this same check to fail on it. |
| `user/terminal.c` | The Terminal, as a ring-3 program and a compositor window: the twenty-fifth app out of the kernel. Same look as before (dark page, scrollback, `> ` prompt pinned to the bottom, block cursor, hint line), drawn with libjt's antialiased DejaVu Sans Mono (`jt_mono_draw`, 8 px advance). It keeps its own working directory, shows it in the prompt, handles `cd` itself and sends the cwd with every `SYS_SHELL_RUN` call. Commands go through the one new call `SYS_SHELL_RUN`; copy, cut and paste use an in-app one-line clipboard. Backquote is the deliberate crash. |
| `kernel/shellsys.c` + `kernel/shellsys.h` | The allowlisted shell behind `SYS_SHELL_RUN` (help echo uptime mem ps ls cat, relative to the caller's cwd; plus a `crash` that exists only under the `panicdesk` boot flag, for the panic check). It is not `run()`: the syscall runs on the 4KB kernel stack with interrupts off, so it uses static buffers and refuses anything that blocks, waits on the network, opens a window or halts, and never moves the desktop's cwd. See the System call ABI section. |
| `kernel/weather_extra.h` | The data side of the 2.10.0 Weather window, split out of `kernel.c` (its line ceiling only goes down): the 7-day and 24-hour statics, the JSON array readers (`json_section_array`, `json_array_clock` for sunrise, sunset and hour stamps), `wx_parse_extras` for everything past the temperature and condition, and `wx_write_extras`, which appends the `press`, `vis`, `isday`, `d` and `hs/ht/hc/hp/hd` lines to `WEATHER.TXT`. Included by `kernel/weather.c` just before `weather_fetch_inner`. |
| `kernel/stocks.h` | The data side of Stocks, now that the app is `user/stocks.c`: the quote table, the strict row parser, `stocks_fetch` (heap buffers, `/api/stocks?range=N`), `stocks_write_file` leaving `STOCKS.TXT` for the app, and `stocks_ring3_open`, the blocking path (phone grid, Apps folder) that fetches once and runs the app once. The window refetches through `SYS_REFRESH`. |
| `kernel/clockicon.h` | The Clock tile as a live analog face: white dial, ticks and hour, minute and centre hands drawn in physical pixels over the plain cached tile, redrawn when the minute changes. Also the one hook that lays Clock's hands on a tile; Calendar is plain art with nothing drawn over it. |
| `user/search.c` | Search, the query box that filters the current directory as you type, as a ring-3 program: the eighteenth app out of the kernel (1.9.13). Same list, live filter, folder step-in and cat-style file view as the old in-kernel copy. It needed one new call, `SYS_READDIR`, which fills fixed-size records (name, size, is_dir) for a directory named by a path relative to the shell's directory; the kernel walks the path and walks back inside the call, so the app keeps its own cwd string and nothing outside it moves. `SYS_OPEN` takes the same relative paths, so a file inside a folder opens as `DOCS/NAME`. The records and the file buffer live in the zeroed pages past the image. Backquote is the deliberate crash. Driven by `tools/checks/ring3search-check.py`. |
| `user/burrow.c` | Burrow, the Files app, as a ring-3 program: a List and Icons toolbar, folders first, arrows to move, Enter to open a folder, Backspace to go up, Esc to close. It keeps its own folder path and lists it through `SYS_READDIR`. |
| `user/epiphany.c` | Epiphany, the markets terminal (a 40-ticker watchlist, portfolio, trading simulator, macro brief and the `/` command bar for `AAPL GP` and `AAPL DES`), as a ring-3 program: the nineteenth app out of the kernel (1.9.19). Prices come through `SYS_HTTP_GET` from `/api/quotes` (about 700 bytes, inside the 2 KB body), with the compiled-in prices when the fetch fails. The GP chart draws previous close to last price, because the intraday series lives in the kernel's Stocks and does not fit the syscall body. `tools/checks/ring3epiphany-check.py` drives it. |
| `user/weather_pi.c`, `tools/checks/arm64-weather-check.py` | Weather on the ARM64 build (3.16.0), a small ring-3 program the Makefile builds as `weather` (the dock tile named Weather, Spotlight and Samantha's `[[open weather]]` find it by that name). City, the temperature large, a flat condition glyph from discs and capsules, high and low, and five day cards. It reads `WEATHER.TXT` (the format `user/weather.c` documents) through `jt_readfile`; with no file, or `have 0`, it draws a Vancouver sample and says "Sample, not a live reading". There is no live fetch on ARM yet, so it never calls anything live. Check: opens it from the tile and from Spotlight in headless QEMU, finds the temperature ink, the terracotta sun and the five cards in a screendump, and closes it with Esc and the red dot. |
| `user/weather.c` | Weather, as a ring-3 program: the twentieth app out of the kernel (1.9.22), made much richer in 2.10.0. The kernel's `weather_fetch` still does the network work, in one Open-Meteo request (current, 24 hours, 7 days with sunrise, sunset, UV and chance of rain), and writes `WEATHER.TXT` after every fetch (scalars, `d` lines per day, `hs/ht/hc/hp/hd` lines for the hours); the app reads it with plain file calls and shows the live reading, the last good one or labelled sample data in four sections (overview, hourly strip, 7-day bars, details), moved with Tab and the arrow keys. It is a real compositor window: R draws Fetching and calls `SYS_REFRESH` (398), the desktop loop refetches outside the gate, and the app reloads when `jt_sysinfo.data_stamp` moves. `tools/checks/ring3weather-check.py` drives it, `tools/checks/weather-app-check.sh` covers the live, stale, bad, timeout and offline faces. |
| `user/stocks.c` | Stocks, the watchlist sidebar with sparklines and a five-range line chart, as a ring-3 program: the twenty-first app out of the kernel. The kernel's `stocks_fetch` still does the network work and writes `STOCKS.TXT` after every fetch; the app reads the whole file with `jt_readfile` (one `jt_read` moves only 255 bytes). Arrow keys and clicks pick a symbol, a range change or R calls `SYS_REFRESH` (398) with `range | sel << 8`, the desktop loop refetches, and the window reloads when `data_stamp` moves. It is a real compositor window; Esc or a crash returns to the desktop. |
| `user/portfolio.c` | Portfolio, Joshua's About block and the fleet catalog grouped Life, Read, Make, Play and Dev, as a ring-3 program: the eleventh app out of the kernel (1.9.5). Same rows as the old in-kernel copy, up/down or the wheel skip the group headers, a click selects, and the bottom line shows the selected app's address. Backquote is the deliberate crash `tools/checks/ring3portfolio-check.py` can press. |
| `drivers/user_activity.h`, `user_calendar.h`, `user_clock.h`, `user_curbfind.h`, `user_epiphany.h`, `user_search.h`, `user_contacts.h`, `user_fieldbook.h`, `user_tonchi.h`, `user_portfolio.h`, `user_reminders.h`, `user_hikko.h` | The compressed bytes (raw deflate, with the raw length and an Adler-32) of those ring-3 apps, embedded in the kernel so it can start them; `ring3app_seed` unpacks and checks them at launch. Generated by `tools/gen/gen_user_bin.py` on every build, ignored by git, never edited by hand. |
| `drivers/user_brkpoke.h`, `user_burrow.h`, `user_calculator.h`, `user_keyrate.h`, `user_mail.h`, `user_hamurabi.h`, `user_windgate.h`, `user_movies.h`, `user_music.h`, `user_notes.h`, `user_samantha.h`, `user_stocks.h`, `user_terminal.h`, `user_weather.h` | The same, for the rest of the ring-3 apps and the break-on-purpose test program: compressed bytes embedded in the kernel, generated by `tools/gen/gen_user_bin.py` on every build, ignored by git, never edited by hand. |
| `kernel/ring3app.c` + `kernel/ring3app.h` | The table-driven launcher and supervisor for apps that run as ring-3 processes (`RING3_APPS`: name, embedded binary, VFS filename). Seeds the binary onto the VFS, runs it with `exec_user`, and when it exits or is reaped after a fault, logs what happened and hands the desktop back. Since 1.9.23 also `ring3app_launch_window`, the non-blocking launch behind a compositor window (see Ring-3 windows below). |
| `tools/checks/ring3window-check.py` | 1.9.23: Reminders as a ring-3 compositor window beside Notes. Both on screen, the crash key only reaches the focused window, and the crash closes only its own window. |
| `kernel/irqlock.h` | 1.9.23: `irq_save` / `irq_restore`, the one lock a uniprocessor kernel needs. A cli critical section that hands back the caller's own IF, used by kheap, vfs, the window event ring, task_kill and the window launcher. |
| `kernel/r3stress.c` | 1.9.23: the `stress=r3` boot flag. The desktop churns the heap and writes inside NOTES/ every frame while every SYS_WINDOW_POLL from a ring-3 window does the same under the gate; after 400 frames it walks the heap and reports to serial. Dead code unless armed. |
| `tools/checks/ring3stress-check.py` | 1.9.23: boots `open=remi stress=r3` on a real FAT disk, asserts both sides ran, no block was handed to two owners, `kheap: ok`, the syscall's file stayed at the root, the desktop finished. The lockless kernel hangs inside the first seconds. |
| `tools/checks/pdesync-check.py` | 1.9.24: boots `open=remi stress=pde`, grows the kernel heap into a 4 MB region that had no page table when the ring-3 task was created, has the task write and read it through SYS_WINDOW_POLL on its own CR3, then closes it with Esc. Without `paging_sync_task_dirs` it logs the BUG line and faults. |

#### Storage

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `drivers/ata.c` | Reads and writes the hard disk. |
| `drivers/blockdev.c` | One `read_sector` / `write_sector` table so the filesystem does not care whether the bytes come from a disk or from RAM. |
| `drivers/ramdisk.c` | The RAM-backed block device. |
| `drivers/fat.c` | FAT16 with real subdirectories, writes and 8.3 names. Since 2.53.0 also FAT32 reads (28-bit FAT entries, the root as a cluster chain, the high half of each first cluster), for the Pi's boot partition, and since 2.57.0 FAT32 writes: 32-bit cluster numbers, every FAT copy, the top four bits of each entry kept, `first_cluster_high` set, FSInfo's free count marked unknown on the first change. A delete frees its clusters on both. The ARM build links the same file against `arch/arm64/emmc.c`. Since 2.58.0 `fat_cwd_get` and `fat_cwd_set` hand out the one current directory, so `arch/arm64/ufile.c` can walk an app's path and put it back. A folder still never grows past its first cluster, and no entry gets a timestamp. Crash safe since 4.13.0: a replaced file's new data and chain are written to fresh clusters first, one directory-sector write flips the entry, and only then are the old clusters freed (delete, rmdir and mkdir keep the same shape), so a pulled plug leaves the old file or the new one. `fat_mount` then runs `fat_recover`: it marks every cluster reachable from the root, copies the first FAT over any other copy that differs, frees clusters used but reachable from no entry, and writes FSInfo's free count exact; it changes nothing if the tree cannot be read whole or the volume has more clusters than the scan bitmap covers (1M on the Pi, 128K on i386). `USERS.TXT` takes the same path through `sd_write`. |
| `drivers/ramfs.c` | A tiny flat in-memory filesystem, eight files of 4KB. Exists so the VFS has a second backend to prove it abstracts anything. |
| `drivers/vfs.c` | The filesystem switch. Every app and every shell command goes through here instead of calling FAT directly. |
| `drivers/trash.c` | Recoverable delete. `rm` moves a file here; you can restore it from the Trash on the desktop. Lives in RAM, so it empties on reboot. |

#### Network

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `drivers/pci.c` | Walks the PCI bus so the kernel can find its own network card. |
| `drivers/rtl8139.c` | Driver for the RTL8139 card, QEMU's default. |
| `drivers/ne2k.c` | Driver for the NE2000 card, which is what the browser emulator provides. The kernel probes for RTL8139 first and falls back to this. |
| `drivers/net.c` | Ethernet, ARP, IPv4, UDP, DNS and TCP, built up from raw frames on top of whichever card was found (on ARM, the card behind `drivers/nic.h`). `net_init` leases a real address by DHCP (DISCOVER/OFFER/REQUEST/ACK) first, falling back to the old fixed config if nothing answers; `nodhcp` on the command line skips it. `udp_broadcast_ask` sends one UDP broadcast on DHCP's frame path and returns the first reply: how the Pi finds the Claude relay. |
| `drivers/http.c` | `http_get` and `http_post` over that TCP. Plain HTTP only. There is no TLS. |
| `drivers/html.c` | A deliberately tiny HTML-to-text converter, enough to read a page or a ported app. |
| `drivers/json.c` | A small JSON reader for the weather, geolocation and chat responses. |

The network stack is what fetches the weather in the menu bar, the map
tiles for the wallpaper, stock quotes, and the replies from a local
Ollama server in the Chat app.

#### Sound

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `drivers/sb16.c` | The Sound Blaster 16 driver: DSP reset/detect, IRQ 5, and ISA DMA channel 1 for both directions. `sb16_play`/`sb16_beep` push 8-bit unsigned mono PCM out through the SB16-only high-speed command pair (`0x41` set rate, `0xC0` transfer). `sb16_record` captures the same format in, through the older DSP-2.xx-compatible ADC pair (`0x40` set time constant, `0x24` transfer) every real SB16 answers -- QEMU's own `-device sb16` has no ADC path today (confirmed against its source), so recording only produces real audio on hardware, or under `make talk`'s coreaudio backend. |
| `drivers/speak.c` | Text to speech: posts to Turing's `/api/speak`, plays the PCM reply on `sb16.c`, and tracks its own playback position so Chat's face can move with it (`speak_level`). |

#### Worker endpoints

The browser landing page routes kernel HTTP requests to a Cloudflare Worker
(`worker.js`) for external APIs:

| Endpoint | What it does |
|---|---|
| `/api/stocks` | Live stock quotes for the Stocks app. |
| `/api/books` | The real Bookrank shelf (top twelve ranked books) as plain text for the Bookrank app. |
| `/api/hikko` | The real Thinkpond forum's top twelve ideas as plain text (`votes|category|title|text|plan`), ASCII only, read-only, for the Thinkpond app. |
| `/api/lexly` | The real Patois (was Patois, was Lexly) course list as plain text, and with `?c=<id>` up to twenty four-choice questions from one course, ASCII only, for the Patois app. |
| `/api/chat` | LLM routing and chat responses for the Chat app. |
| `/api/pick` | LLM tool selection for Chat's command execution. |
| `/api/waitlist` | Dev-kit waitlist signup (POST) and count (GET). Stores one email per address with a timestamp. |
| `/api/listen` | Speech-to-text for Chat's push-to-talk: runs Workers AI Whisper over a posted audio clip. |

#### Graphics and input

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `drivers/vbe.c` | Switches the screen into 1920x1080 graphics. |
| `drivers/window.c` | The one drawing target everything renders through: pixels, rectangles, a viewport, and a partial-repaint band. |
| `drivers/font.c` | Reads the real 8x16 IBM font out of VGA hardware at boot. Falls back to an embedded copy when the read comes back empty, as it does in the browser. |
| `drivers/png.c` + `drivers/jpeg.c` | Small image decoders, 8-bit RGB and RGBA PNG and baseline JPEG. The wallpaper map tiles go through PNG. |
| `drivers/inflate.c` + `drivers/inflate.h` | The kernel's one DEFLATE inflate (plus Adler-32). The PNG decoder and the app unpacker in `kernel/ring3app.c` both use it, so there is a single copy to trust. |
| `tools/inflate-host/main.c` | Host harness for `drivers/inflate.c`. `tools/checks/user-compress-check.py` builds it with every app header and requires each embedded app to inflate to its exact binary and every damaged copy to be refused. |
| `drivers/ttf.c` | A runtime TrueType rasterizer, so text scales to any size. Wraps `drivers/stb_truetype.h`, vendored unmodified from Sean Barrett's stb, with the heap and string shims it needs. |
| `drivers/dejavu_font.h` and siblings | The six DejaVu faces, Sans, Serif and Mono in regular and bold, as ASCII plus Latin-1 subsets: `dejavu_bold_font.h`, `dejavu_serif_font.h`, `dejavu_serif_bold_font.h`, `dejavu_mono_font.h`, `dejavu_mono_bold_font.h`. Generated from the TTFs in `tools/fonts/`. |
| `drivers/lib_inter_font.h` and siblings | The font library: eight open faces (Inter, EB Garamond, Comic Neue, Caveat Brush, Dancing Script, JetBrains Mono, Source Serif, Source Sans) as ASCII subsets, registered by name in `drivers/ttf.c` and listed by the `fonts` shell command. Sources and licences: the Type system section. |
| `drivers/mouse.c` | The PS/2 mouse, including the wheel. |
| `drivers/vmmouse.c` | The VMware absolute-pointer interface. When the host answers, a tap lands exactly where the finger is. Real hardware falls back to `mouse.c`. |
| `drivers/serial.c` | COM1 output for debugging. A boot trace you can read with `-serial stdio` even after the screen is dead. |
| `kernel/console.h` | The three text-console functions the kernel uses before the desktop exists. |

The desktop is laid out at 960x540 and drawn at 1920x1080. Every ordinary
pixel write fills a 2x2 block, so apps never think about scaling. Icons,
text and the wallpaper write physical pixels, which is why they are sharp.
There is no double buffer; the kernel repaints only the region that
changed.

#### The desktop

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `kernel/wallmap.c` + `kernel/wallmap.h` | The map and satellite wallpapers, out of kernel.c since 4.18.3: the tile fetch around the place `geo_*` names (OpenTopoMap PNGs or Google satellite JPEGs, `tilehost=` for fake servers in checks), the theme switch, the baked satellite fallback in `kernel/wall_sat.h` (only this file includes it), and `wall_apply`, which points `wall_src` at what the desktop draws. The per-pixel readers stay in kernel.c, inlined in the draw loops; the 1.5 MB photo stays there too, reached here through `wall_photo`. |
| `kernel/settings.c` + `kernel/settings.h` | `SETTINGS.TXT`, out of kernel.c since 4.18.4: `settings_load` once at boot (every key bounds-checked, a missing or corrupt file leaves the defaults) and `settings_save` on every change, plus the LLM, Mail and Claude relay settings the file carries. Settings' window, the shell and kmain's command-line overrides use the state directly; `SYS_HTTP_POST` reads it through the getters. |
| `kernel/selftest.c` + `kernel/selftest.h` | The shell's built-in self-tests that need no kernel.c state, out of `run()` since 4.18.5: `pngtest` and `jpegtest` (with their baked fixtures, `png_testdata.h` and `jpeg_testdata.h`), `heaptest`, `nettest` and `mailtest` (with `mail.h`, its one includer). `run()` hands every line to `selftest_run` first. |
| `kernel/taskdemo.c` | The scheduler demos, out of `run()` since 4.18.6: `tasktest`, `fputest`, `preempttest`, `killtest`, `spawntest`, `isotest` and `reaptest`, and the small tasks they start. Each proves one thing about `task.c` (switching, per-task x87 state, preemption, kill, private pages, slot reuse). |
| `kernel/shellfs.c` | The shell's file commands, out of `run()` since 4.18.7: `ls`, `cat`, `write`, `rm` (into the trash first), `trash`, `restore`, `cd`, `mkdir`, the text-mode `browse`, and `fsuse`/`diskuse` to switch the filesystem or disk underneath. `exec` stays in kernel.c with the bare-name fallthrough it shares. |
| `kernel/weather.c` + `kernel/weather.h` | The menu-bar weather, out of kernel.c since 4.12.3: ip-api (or `loc=` through Open-Meteo's geocoder) for the place, Open-Meteo for the reading, the error states the Weather window names, the rain and snow particles, and `WEATHER.TXT` for `user/weather.c`. The header lists what the rest of the kernel reads and what weather.c borrows back (the cmdline overrides, the wind level, the menu bar's redraw). |
| `kernel/kernel.c` | The big one. The text console, the keyboard scancode table, the clock, the shell, and the whole desktop: menu bar, dock, windows, Apps folder, Burrow (the file browser), Settings, Lock Screen, the wind-swayed tree. Most apps are still drawn from here. |
| `kernel/ttf_render.h` | The shared glyph path for anything drawing real DejaVu text at physical resolution: a per-face cache, a glyph cache, the antialiased ink blend. Notes and the Terminal both draw through it. |
| `kernel/gui_prims.c` | The x87 square root the antialiased lines use. i386 only. |
| `kernel/gui_paint.c`, `kernel/gui_paint.h` | The desktop painters both builds share (2.22.0): colour blend and lerp, the rounded rect on the wallpaper, the dock tray and its hairline, the icon contact shadow, the icon artwork scaler and the tile blit, plus the default dock order. Since 2.23.0 also the circle and capsule primitives every icon glyph uses, the dock's hover label and the app window frame (rounded body, traffic lights, centred title). Since 2.24.0 also the software cursor: the save-under and the antialiased arrow. Integer only, so the ARM build links the same file. It draws through `window.h`'s calls, `gui_wallpaper_sample` and the `gui_text` pair; `drivers/window.c` and `font.c` answer them on i386, `arch/arm64/main.c` over the framebuffer on the Pi. |
| `kernel/dock_geom.c` | Dock geometry and hit-testing: where each icon sits at the current scale, and which slot a click landed on. The ARM build links it too. |
| `kernel/dock_draw.c` | The i386 dock's animation: the band cache behind hover and drag, and the icons. The tray, shadow and hover label come from `gui_paint.c`, the layout from `dock_geom.c`. |
| `kernel/apps_geom.h` | Where the Launchpad (Apps folder) puts its glass panel, icons and labels. Pure integer math, centered on any screen, rows drop to fit a short one. |
| `kernel/hint.h` | The keyboard-hint line every app draws ("esc closes" and friends), skipped on phones where there is no keyboard. Split out of `kernel.c`. |
| `kernel/app.h` | The app interface. One `struct app` per app (name, tile color, glyph, `open`, and `draw`/`key` for apps that run in a desktop window), plus the few desktop services and helpers an app in its own file needs. |
| `kernel/app.c` | The helpers behind `app.h`, only ones two or more apps were writing by hand: start an app's window with its titlebar, print a number. |
| `kernel/bench.h` | Built-in benchmarks: boot time, heap, memcpy, context switch, disk read. `bench` in the shell or on the command line. Results in the Benchmarks section. |
| `kernel/auth.h` | User accounts. PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 records in `USERS.TXT` (scheme tag and iteration count stored per line), verify-only support for the pre-1.7.9 chained-SHA-256 records with an in-place upgrade on the next successful login, the login and first-run screens. Built against the Threat model section. |
| `kernel/auth_kdf.c` + `kernel/auth_kdf.h` | PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 (RFC 8018) as one block loop over BearSSL's `br_hmac`; no primitive of its own. Compiled for the kernel and natively for `tools/auth-host`, which pins it to the RFC 7914 and RFC 6070 (SHA-256) vectors. |
| `kernel/wall_sat.h` | A real satellite photo, baked in, used as the wallpaper when there is no network to fetch map tiles. |
| `arch/arm64/apple_mask.h` | The apple after the tribute line on the ARM64 desktop: a 48 x 48 alpha mask of a bitten apple with a leaf, box-filtered from a 512 px drawing made of circles and a leaf lens. `main.c` scales it to three and a half times the line's radius with 4x4 samples a pixel. |
| `user/stocks_pi.c`, `tools/checks/arm64-stocks-check.py` | Stocks on the ARM64 build (3.18.0), a small ring-3 program the Makefile builds as `stocks` (the dock tile named Stocks, Spotlight and Samantha's `[[open stocks]]` find it by that name). A watchlist on the left (symbol, name, price, a green or red change pill, a trend line) and an anti-aliased line chart of the selected symbol on the right, Up and Down to pick. It reads `STOCKS.TXT` (the format `user/stocks.c` documents) through `jt_readfile`; with no file it draws a built-in sample and says "Sample, not live prices". There is no fetch on ARM yet, so it never claims a live quote. Check: opens it from the tile and from Spotlight in headless QEMU, finds the pills and the chart line in a screendump, presses Down and sees the chart change, and closes it with Esc and the red dot. |
| `kernel/boot_mark.h` | The brand mark as 8-bit alpha coverage, written by `tools/gen/gen_boot_mark.py`: `landing/logo.svg` (the crayon tree) at the splash's physical size, blended onto the boot screen by `gui_draw_boot_mark_steps` (`kernel/kernel.c`), together with `boot_mark_rev`, the frame each pixel first appears in as the pen draws the tree on, terracotta ground last (the splash reveals it over `BOOT_DRAW_TICKS`), and `landing/mark-bold.svg` (the clean heavy copy) at 80 px for the menu bar and the About card (`gui_draw_mark_sized`) and the ARM menu bar (`arch/arm64/main.c`). Since 3.0 each copy also carries `*_acc`, the part of each pixel that is the ground, which `gui_blend_cov` and `menu_mark_paint` blend in `MARK_GROUND` (terracotta) and the rest in ink. The SVGs are drawn by `tools/gen/logo.py`; `tools/gen/gen_brand_art.py` rasterizes the PNG copies (`landing/mark.png`, `landing/badge.png`, `landing/og-image.png`, `docs/brand/`). |
| `kernel/phone_home.h` | The phone home screen for `boot_to_phone`: a 5-column, no-scroll grid of all 26 apps, a status bar with the real clock and weather, and a tappable back chevron in place of the desktop's traffic lights (drawn as two bold stepped diagonal strokes, injects a real Esc scancode through `kbd_inject()` so every app closes through the one `kbd_pop()==27` path a keyboard already drives). Desktop mode never calls into it, so it stays pixel-identical. |
| `kernel/settings_ui.h` | Settings app UI with a sidebar plus grouped detail pane (modeled on macOS System Settings). Pulled into its own file in the 1.7.x redesign pass to keep `kernel.c` under the godfile-check.sh ceiling; `#include`d directly into `kernel.c` at the exact spot the inline version used to sit. |

### Ring-3 windows (1.9.23)

Until 1.9.23 a ring-3 program was modal.
- `exec_user` blocked the desktop loop until the program exited.
- The program owned the one app viewport.
- `SYS_WINDOW_POLL` pulled keys straight off the keyboard ring.
- Two programs could not even run at once. Every task directory shares the base page tables by value, so `JT_USER_BASE` and `JT_USER_FB` were the same physical frames in every task.

The window path changes four things.

The desktop never falls back to the blocking path any more. A full window table or failed launch is refused with a notice and a `winrefuse: <App>` serial line (`gui_refuse_open`). The blocking path remains only for the phone home grid, the Apps folder and the text shell.

- `exec_user_window` (kernel/exec.c) loads the image and builds the stack in a kmalloc'd buffer of the window's own. It returns the task id at once.
  - `paging_task_map_private` (kernel/paging.c) gives that task's directory a private copy of the 4 MB page table that holds the user window. The image, stack and framebuffer entries point at frames only this task maps.
  - The low identity alias keeps the shared supervisor-only table, so no task reaches another's pages through it.
  - `paging_user_range_ok` now walks CR3, so a syscall pointer is judged by the caller's own tables.

**Kernel PDEs are shared by write-through, not by copy.**
- A task directory is a by-value copy of the kernel directory taken at `task_create`. That was a trap.
- The heap grows through `paging_map_region`. A 4 MB region that got its page table after the task was born existed only in the shared directory. So the first syscall or IRQ on that task's CR3 to touch it page-faulted at ring 0 (Mail's close inside `r3win_release`).
- Preallocating every kernel table is the textbook fix, but the wrong trade here. The identity map covers physical frames on demand and the LFB sits at 0xFD000000. A full set is 4 MB of BSS against 64 KB today.
- Instead, `kernel/paging.c` treats the shared directory as the one source of truth. `paging_sync_task_dirs` writes every kernel PDE create or remove through to every live task directory on the spot. That costs zero extra memory and a six-slot loop per table change, and table changes happen once per 4 MB.
- The two slots a task owns privately are skipped: `PAGING_PRIVATE_PDE`, and the user window table at `KERNEL_PDE_INDEX + 1`. So `paging_task_map_private` is untouched.
- `paging_check_task_dirs` compares every live copy against the kernel entries. It logs `BUG: paging: task directory drifted` if one differs.
- `stress=pde` (kernel/r3stress.c) and `tools/checks/pdesync-check.py` arm the whole path headlessly.

The other window changes:

- `syscall_window_register` (kernel/syscall.c, `r3wins`) allocates the framebuffer. It maps it at `JT_USER_FB` in that task only, and keeps a 16-deep event ring per window.
  - `SYS_WINDOW_OPEN` hands back the same `jt_window_info` as before.
  - `SYS_WINDOW_POLL` with `JT_POLL_PRESENT` marks the window dirty instead of copying anything, and drains the ring. It returns `-EAGAIN` when the ring is empty.
- The compositor (kernel/kernel.c, `gui_window_t.task`) blits a dirty window's buffer through `window_pixel`, at the window's position, with the usual chrome.
  - It feeds keys and content clicks only to the topmost window's ring (`syscall_window_push_event`).
  - It sends a wheel step to the ring-3 window under the pointer (`JT_EV_WHEEL`). The step is dropped over anything else. Before 2.44.1 no window got one.
  - The red dot kills the task.
  - Every frame it looks for a window whose task is gone (exit, or a fault reaped by idt.c). It logs the same crash line the blocking launcher does, and closes that one window.
- Resize is real (1.10).
  - `ring3app_window_blit` compares the viewport with the window's buffer. `syscall_window_request_size` queues one `JT_EV_RESIZE`.
  - The app re-opens (`jt_window_resized` in user/jtsys.h). `r3win_resize` maps a fresh zeroed buffer with `paging_task_remap_private`.
  - The old buffer is freed on the compositor's next look, so a blit in flight never reads freed memory. Until then the old buffer is blitted clipped.
  - `tools/checks/ring3resize-check.py` proves it.
- `gui_ring3_windowed` names the apps on this path. Reminders is the proof. Adding an app is one row. Burrow, Notes and Mail can move to ring 3 on it without losing multi-window.

**The concurrency rule.** There is one CPU, so there is one rule: shared kernel state is only ever touched with interrupts off.

- A syscall already runs that way end to end, because `int 0x80` is an interrupt gate.
- The only call that turns interrupts back on is `SYS_HTTP_GET`. It holds nothing while it waits. http.c refuses a second fetch while one is in flight, from the desktop or the gate.
- The desktop (task 0) runs with interrupts on and can be preempted anywhere. So every entry point it shares with a syscall is an `irq_save`/`irq_restore` section (kernel/irqlock.h):
  - `kmalloc` and `kfree`
  - every `vfs_*` call (which covers FAT, ramfs and the disk)
  - `syscall_window_push_event` into the per-window ring
  - `task_kill`'s frame patch
  - the whole of `ring3app_launch_window` (a slot is marked used before its private mapping exists)
- The FAT cwd is one global cursor the desktop may have left inside NOTES/. So a syscall path resolves from the root and puts the cursor back (`vfs_cwd_get`/`vfs_cwd_set`).
- `kheap_check` walks the free list on demand. `tools/checks/ring3stress-check.py` hammers both sides at once to keep it that way.

**The memory map.**
- The kernel is linked at 0xC0100000. Its .text, .rodata, .data and .bss run upward from there.
- Every ring-3 app binary is baked into .rodata as raw deflate (the generated `drivers/user_*.h`, about half the raw size since 2.7.1). So each new app pushes .bss higher, only more slowly.
- Above .bss sit three fixed, NOLOAD reservations that `pmm.c` never hands to the heap:
  - the 132 KB program window at `JT_USER_BASE` (`JT_USER_IMAGE_PAGES`, 32 pages of image, plus 1 of stack)
  - the 64 KB Sound Blaster DMA buffer at `JT_DMABUF_BASE`
  - the ring-3 window framebuffer at `JT_USER_FB`
- All three are written down once, in `kernel/memmap.h`. The Makefile turns that header into `boot/memmap.ld`. `boot/linker.ld`, `user/hello.ld` and `user/note.ld` INCLUDE it. So the loader, the reservations and the flat binaries' link address cannot drift.
- They have to stay inside the second 4 MB kernel page table (0xC0400000 to 0xC07FFFFF), because `paging_task_map_private` copies that one table for a task's private window. `boot/linker.ld` asserts that, plus the order and the DMA alignment.
- `kernel/exec.h`, both user link scripts and `drivers/ramfs.c` take the size from the header. ramfs now allocates each file to its actual length instead of the cap.

How the map moved over time:
- On 2026-10-01 the window moved up 512 KB (0xC0507000 to 0xC0587000). `tools/checks/bss-margin-check.py` now reports about 544 KB between .bss and the window, with a 16 KB floor.
- Later that day the image window grew from 7 pages to 32 (28 KB to 128 KB). Samantha and Stocks had both closed to within 1 KB of the old ceiling.
  - A brief detour to 128 pages for Samantha's face frames was undone once `SYS_BRK` landed (see below).
  - `JT_USER_BASE` stayed put. `.dmabuf` and `.userfb` slid up 128 KB (0xC05B0000 and 0xC05C0000, ending at 0xC0730000, still inside the table).
- In 2.5.1, `.dmabuf` and `.userfb` slid back down into the gap under the program window, to 0xC05B0000 and 0xC05C0000. `JT_USER_FB_BYTES` grew to 0x220000 so a 960x540 window fits, ending at 0xC07E0000.
- On 2026-10-04 all three moved up another 64 KB, when Hamurabi's binary took the gap under 16 KB.
  - `JT_USER_BASE` is now 0xC0597000, `.dmabuf` 0xC05C0000 and `.userfb` 0xC05D0000, ending at 0xC07F0000.
  - So 64 KB of the table is left above it, and about 62 KB between .bss and the window.
- Playable Hamurapi then grew its binary from 54 KB to 81 KB. That left about 34 KB, against the 16 KB floor.
- 2.7.1 stored the app binaries compressed. That took the gap to about 438 KB.

**The task heap (1.9.27).** Anything larger than the image window goes through `SYS_BRK` (`kernel/brk.c`).

- Each task's heap starts at `JT_BRK_BASE` (0xFF000000, `kernel/memmap.h`). It grows up to `JT_BRK_MAX_PAGES` (8 MB), in PDEs 0x3FC and 0x3FD.
- That is above every identity alias `paging_map_region` can create. Physical RAM and the 0xFD000000 framebuffer both sit lower.
- It is also far from the private window PDE 19 and the user image, stack, `.dmabuf` and `.userfb` inside the second kernel table. So a task directory snapshot of the kernel's PDEs never collides with it, and nothing the kernel maps later overwrites it.
- Frames come from the PMM one at a time. They are zeroed, and mapped user and writable in that task's directory only.
- The syscall switches to the kernel directory while it edits tables, because a task directory misses pages the kernel mapped after it was made. It switches back after, which also flushes the TLB.
- Exit and crash both reach `brk_release` from `syscall_release_task`. It frees every frame and both page tables, and logs `brk: released N pages, live=M`.
- `user/libjt`'s `malloc` sits on it. Samantha keeps all 72 face JPEGs there (about 1.5 MB), decoding the frame on screen on demand.

### Samantha at ring 3 (1.9.26, done)

Samantha (`user/samantha.c`) was the twenty-sixth and last app inside the kernel. Her old in-kernel chat and face are deleted. `kernel/phone_home.h` stays, because it is the phone's home screen and app grid, the OS shell, not her UI.

She is last because she is the only app that touches everything: the network, the sound card both ways, kernel-only state like the weather fields, and the launcher.

The rule for the move is the same as every port before it:
- the fewest new syscalls that keep the host, the devices and the desktop's launch path on the kernel side
- every user pointer through `paging_user_range_ok`
- nothing that blocks with interrupts off, except around a network wait
- nothing a program can point anywhere the kernel did not choose

| Capability | In the kernel today | At ring 3 |
|---|---|---|
| Chat request and reply (`/api/chat`, `/api/pick`) | `http_post_timeout` to `llm_host:llm_port` with a 6 KB request and an 8 KB reply, 45s and 10s budgets | `SYS_HTTP_POST` (392): one struct argument, path only, host and port stay the Settings-owned `llm_host`/`llm_port`, body and reply bounce through kernel buffers, 6 KB in, 8 KB out, reply budget clamped to 45s. No streaming: the Worker already answers `stream:false`, and a streaming reply would need the gate to hand back partial bodies with IF on, which nothing else does. |
| Audio out (`/api/speak`) | `chat_face_speak` fetches the WAV and `sb16_play`s it, with a progress hook that drives the mouth frames | The program fetches the clip itself with `SYS_HTTP_POST` (the Worker returns 8-bit mono PCM under 8 KB per sentence; longer answers are sent one sentence at a time, the same split `speak.c` already does) and queues it with a new `SYS_AUDIO_PLAY` (393): user PCM copied into the kernel DMA buffer, returns at once, the program polls `SYS_AUDIO_STATUS` (393 with a flag) for the sample position to time the mouth. The kernel never fetches on the program's behalf: that would put a URL and a text body inside the gate for no gain. |
| Audio in (F2 push-to-talk, `/api/listen`) | `sb16_record` in 2s DMA chunks into a kmalloc'd 8s buffer, posted raw | `SYS_AUDIO_RECORD` (394): fills a user buffer with up to `n` samples at 16 kHz, blocks for one chunk with IF on like `http_get` does, returns the count; -ENODEV without a card. The program keeps the 8s cap and posts the samples with `SYS_HTTP_POST` (body cap rises to 128 KB only if the measured upload needs it; the first cut keeps 6 KB and trims the clip). F2 arrives as a normal key event through `SYS_WINDOW_POLL`. |
| Face | the old kernel face code drew frames from baked arrays with the `font`/`window` primitives | Pure app drawing into the window framebuffer with libjt; the frame arrays move into the user binary. Nothing kernel-side. |
| Reminders, notes, mail, calendar | `vfs_*` on REMINDERS.TXT, NOTES/NOTES, MAIL.TXT, EVENTS.TXT | Existing `open`/`read`/`write`/`readdir`/`mkdir` syscalls; these are files, and Reminders, Notes, Mail and Calendar already read and write them from ring 3. No new call. |
| Weather | `weather_text`/`weather_have` kernel fields | `SYS_SYSINFO` (395): fills a small read-only struct (weather text, model name, host name, time) from kernel state. Read only, one struct, one range check. |
| `open <app>` | `chat_launch_after` set, the desktop loop launches through `gui_apps_launch` | `SYS_LAUNCH_REQUEST` (396): the kernel checks the name against `APPS[]` and stores one pending index; the desktop loop (task 0, IF on) picks it up and runs the normal `gui_apps_launch` path. Nothing is launched inside the gate: `ring3app_launch_window` is an irqlock section the desktop owns, and the program only ever asks. |
| Phone mode (`phone`, `samantha` cmdline) | `phone_home.h` draws the home screen in kernel | The same binary with an argv flag (`samantha --phone`), launched by `kmain` through `ring3app` in place of the desktop; the phone home grid moves into the program and uses `SYS_LAUNCH_REQUEST` for its icons. |

Slices, each sized for one Sonnet agent in about ten minutes, in order:

1. `SYS_HTTP_POST` kernel side (done, this section). Sonnet.
2. (done) The samantha.c program skeleton under user/: window, conversation list, keyboard, `/api/chat` and `/api/pick` through `SYS_HTTP_POST`. Sonnet.
3. The tools: reminders, notes, mail, calendar through the file syscalls, moved line for line from `chat_run_tool`. Haiku.
4. `SYS_SYSINFO` + weather tool, `SYS_LAUNCH_REQUEST` + `open <app>`, desktop pickup in the main loop. Sonnet.
5. The face: frame arrays into the user binary, libjt drawing, idle and talking states. Haiku.
6. `SYS_AUDIO_PLAY`/`SYS_AUDIO_STATUS` plus the per-sentence speak path and mouth timing. Sonnet.
7. `SYS_AUDIO_RECORD` plus push-to-talk and `/api/listen`. Sonnet.
8. (done) Ring-3 Samantha is the default. Sonnet.
   - Dock slot 6, the `chat` and `samantha` shell commands, `boot_to_samantha` and phone mode (argv `phone`, as Notes gets it) all launch her.
   - The in-kernel chat and face are deleted.
   - `phone_home.h` stays and opens her through `gui_apps_launch`. 26 of 26.
   - `facehost=` moved to `syscall.c` and now sets where her face and speech are fetched.

Checks that must keep passing (`tools/checks`):
- `samantha-fullscreen-check.py`
  - 2.9.0: fills the screen, glass, fading captions, scrollback, the two typing bugs, the mouth follows the voice, Esc and the red dot.
  - 2.9.1: a second machine watches her blink on her own clock. Every other machine boots with `noblink`.
  - 2.12.1: the room around her in a wide window, and captions that never land on her lips or eyes, measured on the framebuffer.
- `chat-face-check.py`
- `chat-live-check.sh`
- `chat-samantha-check.py`
- `chat-speaks-check.py`
- `chatapp-check.py`
- `chattools-check.py`
- `demochat-check.mjs`
- `face-frames-check.py`
- `facespeak-demo-check.mjs`
- `phone-boot-check.py`
- `phone-reboot-check.mjs`
- `phone-samantha-back-check.py`
- `samantha-boot-check.py`

They grep serial lines (`chattool=`, `chat=`, face frame counts). The ring-3 program must print the same lines through `SYS_WRITE` to the serial fd, so the checks move over untouched, the way the Terminal and Notes ports did.

### The apps

Twenty-six apps live in the Apps folder. The dock pins the ones you reach for most. Each is one header. They come in three shapes.

Renames (2.6.30, and the fleet since):
- Lexly is Tonchi, now Patois.
- Roost is Brick, now Atlin.
- Voxprint is Notate.
- Siftbox is Hagaki, now Mailbag.
- Sparkjar is Hikko, now Thinkpond.
- Windgate is Airwell.
- Healstack is Intake.

Samantha still opens an app by its old name. Patois keeps `lexly.jaybulb.com` and `/api/lexly`, and Thinkpond keeps `hikko.jaybulb.com`, until their own addresses exist. Block Frame still answers at its old `wiretext` address.

**Apps with a file on disk.** It is the same pattern every time:
- a fixed-size array in RAM
- one plain text file on the FAT disk
- written through on every change

There is no Save button.

| App | File | On disk |
|---|---|---|
| Notes | `user/notes.c`, `kernel/editor.h` | Folders and many notes under `NOTES/`; an old `NOTES.TXT` moves in as the first note. Notes is the twenty-fourth ring-3 app; `kernel/editor.h` keeps only that migration, the shared pointer position and the DejaVu glyph tables. A changed note saves itself 1 to 2 seconds after the last key, so the red dot never loses an edit. |
| Reminders | `user/reminders.c`, `kernel/ring3app.c` | `REMINDERS.TXT`, one line per item. The fifteenth ring-3 app (1.9.9); its in-kernel copy is gone. |
| Calendar | `user/calendar.c`, `kernel/ring3app.c` | `EVENTS.TXT`, one line per date. The grid itself is computed from the clock. The seventeenth ring-3 app (1.9.12); its in-kernel copy is gone. |
| Mail | `user/mail.c`, `kernel/mail.h` | `MAIL.TXT`, one line per message. Mail is the twenty-second ring-3 app (a compositor window, compose sheet inside it); `kernel/mail.h` keeps only the data layer Samantha's read_mail and send_mail use, reloaded from disk on every call. Two starter messages ship compiled in. |
| Contacts | `user/contacts.c`, `kernel/ring3app.c` | `CONTACTS.TXT`. The thirteenth ring-3 app (1.9.7); its in-kernel copy is gone. |
| Samantha | `user/samantha.c`, `kernel/ring3app.c` | `CHAT.TXT`. Talks to the Worker through `SYS_HTTP_POST`. The twenty-sixth ring-3 app (1.9.26); the in-kernel chat is gone. Details below the table. |

**Samantha, in detail.**

Push-to-talk:
- Holding F2 records through `SYS_AUDIO_RECORD`.
- It posts the clip to the Worker's `/api/listen` (Cloudflare Workers AI Whisper).
- The recognized text runs through the same path a typed message takes.

Full screen since 2.9.0:
- Her 736 pixel portrait fills the window. It is `landing/face/hd.jpg`, one fetch of 59 KB through the Worker's `/face/` route.
- A tall window, the phone, shows all of her edge to edge.
- Since 2.12.1 a wide window gives her room. The portrait is 86 percent of the width, with her eyes about 40 percent down. The sides are her own wall colour, taken from the portrait's top corners. The portrait's outer tenth fades into it.
- The input line is a blurred glass panel.
- What is said shows as captions that rise from just above the input. Up to three stack, newest at the bottom, older ones dimmer. They fade all together after about four quiet seconds.
- In a wide window, her mouth is low and her chin travels down toward the panel. So there is one compact row only, and never over her lips or eyes.
- Tab opens the whole conversation.
- A red dot or Esc closes her.
- Her mouth is drawn by code from the loudness of the PCM she is playing.
- A host without the portrait leaves the old 320 pixel idle and talk frames, scaled up.

**Apps with nothing to save.**

| App | File | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| Stocks | `kernel/stocks.h` | Eight fixed symbols with live quotes and charts from the Worker at `/api/stocks`; the UI is `user/stocks.c`. |
| Epiphany | `user/epiphany.c`, `kernel/ring3app.c` | Watchlist, portfolio, simulator and a Bloomberg-style command bar (`/`, then `AAPL GP` or `AAPL DES`), live prices through `SYS_HTTP_GET`. The nineteenth ring-3 app (1.9.19); its in-kernel copy is gone. |
| Search | `user/search.c`, `kernel/ring3app.c` | Filters the current directory as you type. Enter opens a folder or shows a file. Scoped to what the VFS can list, no whole-disk index. The eighteenth ring-3 app (1.9.13); its in-kernel copy is gone. |
| Portfolio | `user/portfolio.c`, `kernel/ring3app.c` | A catalog of every app in the fleet with its URL. The eleventh ring-3 app (1.9.5); its in-kernel copy is gone. |
| Activity | `user/activity.c`, `kernel/ring3app.c` | Activity Monitor over the real scheduler and memory counters. Refreshes on a timer, can kill a task. The twelfth ring-3 app (1.9.6); its in-kernel copy is gone. |
| Clock | `user/clock.c`, `kernel/ring3app.c` | Current time from the RTC, a countdown timer you can start and pause, and an alarm. The tenth ring-3 app (1.9.4); its in-kernel copy is gone. |
| Music | `user/music.c`, `kernel/ring3app.c` | Plays the `.WAV` and `.MP3` songs in Files and in a `MUSIC` folder. Keeps nothing on disk. The twenty-fifth ring-3 app (2.2), in the Apps folder. |
| Movies | `user/movies.c`, `kernel/ring3app.c` | Plays motion-JPEG AVI clips from Files with sound, pause, seek and fullscreen. In the Apps folder, opened with `open=movi`. A ring-3 app from the start (2.2). |

**Apps ported from the fleet.** Each is a native rewrite of one of the sibling web apps, kept small on purpose.

| App | File | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| Curbfind | `user/curbfind.c`, `kernel/ring3app.c` | Craigslist deals near you, ranked by score, live through `SYS_HTTP_GET` with Vancouver samples when offline. The sixteenth ring-3 app (1.9.11); its in-kernel copy is gone. |
| Patois | `user/tonchi.c`, `kernel/ring3app.c` | Learn anything: pick a live course, then a four-choice drill with a score. Falls back to the Spanish deck when offline (2.6.27). The seventh ring-3 app (1.9.1); its in-kernel copy is gone. |
| Fieldbook | `user/fieldbook.c`, `kernel/ring3app.c` | Every field of science and math, explained plainly. The ninth ring-3 app (1.9.3); its in-kernel copy is gone. |
| Thinkpond | `user/hikko.c`, `kernel/ring3app.c` | The idea forum: the top ideas from the real forum, vote on them for the session only. Falls back to ten demo ideas when offline (2.6.29). The fourteenth ring-3 app (1.9.8); its in-kernel copy is gone. Renamed from Sparkjar (briefly Hotaru) in 2.6.29; Samantha still opens it by either old name. |
| Keyrate | `user/keyrate.c`, `kernel/ring3app.c` | Typing test with endless random words and a live words-per-minute count. The first app running outside the kernel as a ring-3 process (1.7.7); its in-kernel copy is gone. |
| Toroid | `user/toroid.c`, `kernel/ring3app.c` | Conway's Life on a torus. The second ring-3 app (1.7.11); its in-kernel copy is gone. |
